- 2026-07-17 (1) — **Effort-recommendation feature (GEN-466), continuation: shipped Part 1 (the rule); deferred Part 2 (the hook). - 2026-07-15 (1) — **GEN-430 (flash-free + AV-safe backup) — the fix's approach was CHOSEN, built as working copies, and code-reviewed (FIX-FIRST); NOT installed. Full pick-up context logged to GEN-430; status set In Progress. (Opus 4.8, high effort on design/review.)** Continuation of the 07-14 (4) flash saga. Erez rejected the throttle-only path ("once every few minutes is too much and isn't accepted" — a throttle can only cap the flash at ~1/window, never eliminate it). **Root-caused the flash to DETACHMENT** (documented: Node `detached:true` → Win32 `DETACHED_PROCESS` → `CREATE_NO_WINDOW`/`windowsHide` IGNORED, so a detached child always gets a console window). So the fix must run the sweep **inline/attached**, meaning the SessionStart hook **waits** for it (SessionStart hooks block startup synchronously — verified via docs; exit code doesn't block the session; multiple SessionStart hooks run in parallel). Verified on-machine that `spawnSync('git',['--version'],{windowsHide:true})` (attached, not detached) runs windowless in 39ms, and that `-ExecutionPolicy RemoteSigned -Command "& 'local.ps1'"` runs an unsigned LOCAL script (MOTW-free), and that the installed sweep currently carries NO Mark-of-the-Web. Confirmed the scheduled-tasks MCP is the Claude-app scheduler (not Windows Task Scheduler) and there is NO Windows/Claude scheduled task driving the backup — only the SessionStart hook. **Design decided WITH Erez (one question at a time): "Option 2 plain"** — (1) rewrite the hook to run the sweep inline/attached/windowless via `spawnSync('powershell.exe', …, {windowsHide:true})`, NO cmd/start, NO wscript, NO `-EncodedCommand`, NO `-ExecutionPolicy Bypass` (avoids both Avast triggers by construction; uses `RemoteSigned`); (2) a **5-min throttle** on a shared `~/.claude/backup-sweep-state/last-backup` timestamp stamped **up-front on ATTEMPT** (Erez explicitly chose "plain" attempt-semantics over the success-stamping refinement — accepted residual: on a RARE failed upload the offsite copy can be ≤5 min staler; work never lost since the sweep snapshots the full tree; real failures still hit the failure queue), fail-toward-sweeping on any missing/corrupt/future/backward-clock timestamp; (3) short `-NetTimeoutSeconds 8` on the session-start path (wrap uses default 90s) so a bad network can't hang startup; (4) **also run the sweep at `/wrap`** (Erez's request), inline, never throttled, sharing the same timestamp; (5) 5-min hardcoded (Erez: no user-facing config needed). Answered Erez's "why does session-start capture other sessions' work?" — because the sweep snapshots ALL project repos' full working trees as they are on disk, not this session's diff, so any session-start captures cross-session work; the only gap is work done after the last backup with no later trigger, which the frequent starts + the new wrap sweep cover. **Built 3 working copies (scratchpad, NOT installed):** `sessionstart-backup-spawner.working.js` (inline rewrite + throttle; node --check clean), `backup-sweep.working.ps1` (+`-NetTimeoutSeconds` param default 90 threaded to both `Invoke-GitNet` calls; parses clean), `wrap-sweep-step.draft.md` (proposed wrap Step 5b). **Ran the FULL `/vet-code` gate through Step 3:** design `/check` iterated to convergence across multiple rounds (the panels progressively forced the design from a naive throttle → a Stop-hook catch-up → the inline local+bounded-network split; one round-2 holistic reviewer FABRICATED a "login-scheduled backup task exists" claim that would have flipped my decision — caught it by listing scheduled tasks (none exist), a live "don't defer to a reviewer assertion; verify load-bearing claims" catch); then **both mandatory code-review passes** — Pass A (in-session `/code-review` HIGH, 2 finder sub-agents) + Pass B (cold independent `check-reviewer` on Opus). **Result FIX-FIRST — three reviewers triangulated on real defects** (none installed): (1) the hard-timeout is the wrong primary bound — a SIGKILL mid-sweep skips the sweep's `finally` cleanup (leaks local pin refs that block gc + temp index) AND writes NO failure-queue record (silent to the surfacer, breaking the never-silent invariant) → fix by adding an INTERNAL overall-deadline to the sweep (clean skip-and-queue via normal paths) + have the JS hook write its own durable record on a spawnSync kill; (2) `RemoteSigned` fails-closed if the sweep ever acquires a Mark-of-the-Web (plausible via the Google-Drive sync) → keep RemoteSigned + defensively `Unblock-File` the script at hook start (sidesteps needing to know whether plain Bypass-without-EncodedCommand trips Avast); (3) use `stdio:['ignore','ignore','ignore']` (drop maxBuffer overflow-kill path); (4) clean up the `<pid>.json` payload after spawnSync; (5) scrub stale "fire-and-forget/detached/never-blocks" comments. Reviewers CONFIRMED clean: PS param binding, disjoint state-file namespaces, throttle fails-toward-sweeping in all branches (a throttled session doesn't re-stamp, so no self-perpetuating skip), and — critically — **all Avast-flagged patterns absent**. The deduped fix list is saved as `merged-fixlist-v2.md` and copied into GEN-430. **Remaining (next session): apply fixes 1–7, RE-RUN both code-review passes (fix #1 enlarges the .ps1 change), then live-verify incl. the mandatory "Avast stays quiet after install" check, show Erez + approve, mint pass, apply, verify installed; separately apply the wrap Step 5b (skill edit, not gated but synced).** Set **[GEN-430](https://app.notion.com/p/39d6e495d07c817f981bd6eafcf9eeac) → In Progress** directly (primary session ticket, now actively mid-build; title still accurate, no rewrite needed) and appended a complete self-contained pick-up section (decision, working-copy inventory, vetting progress, the 7-item fix list, remaining steps, config knobs) — both writes verified via re-fetch. Classifier-unavailability recurred repeatedly all session (the GEN-394 transient — blocked review sub-agent launches and several ScheduleWakeup/Bash calls, each cleared on retry; NOT the GEN-316 second instance — no sanctioned call was reason-denied — handled silently each turn). No code shipped (all working copies); no `/wrap` run (Erez asked only to log context to Notion + log history). Unresolved items filed: none (the entire continuation is captured on GEN-430).
- 2026-07-14 (4) — **Tried to eliminate the session-start PowerShell window flash from the GEN-382 backup spawner; built a fully-vetted `wscript`+`.vbs`+`-EncodedCommand` hidden-launch replacement, installed it, and it was promptly blocked by Avast (`IDP.HELU.PSE80` on `BACKUP-SPAWN-*.VBS`) — REVERTED to the working cmd/start launcher. Net: no code change shipped (reverted to baseline); three follow-up tickets + one GEN-58 new-element logged.** Erez reported the PowerShell popup as "a terrible experience," firing "after typing into a session and moving to another session." Investigated: no Windows Scheduled Task and no Claude scheduled task drives it — the only spawner is the `sessionstart-backup-spawner.js` SessionStart hook. **Root-caused the flash: it is caused by DETACHMENT** — the backup must survive the hook exiting (fire-and-forget), so it launches detached, and a detached process is severed from the harness's piped stdio so Windows gives it a fresh console window (the flash). By contrast `sync.ps1` at `/wrap` never flashes because it runs inline/attached (verified a tool-run PS has `MainWindowHandle == 0`). Designed a windowless fix (wscript GUI-subsystem host → hidden PowerShell), ran it through the FULL `/vet-code` flow: `/check` converged (2 rounds); **code review across THREE launch-design iterations** — v1 inline `-Command` in VBS (Pass A found a critical `%VAR%`-expansion + inner-quote reopening of the `&%^` hazard for sessionId/hostname), v2 temp `.ps1` + `-File` (Pass A found the running `-File` script can't self-delete — PS holds an open handle → swallowed sharing violation → `.ps1` leaks; **empirically confirmed**; Pass B had wrongly PASSed it — the live test refuted the confident review), v3 `-EncodedCommand` base64 (inert alphabet, no temp `.ps1`) which passed both passes. Live-verified v3 end-to-end (pushed a real backup ref with an adversarial `%TEST%` sessionId intact; `.vbs` self-deleted). Minted the pass, installed, verified byte-identical. **Minutes later Avast blocked the generated `.vbs`** (`IDP.HELU.PSE80`) → backup silently stopped (no failure-queue entry: AV kill happens after wscript launch so `child.on('error')` never fires — exactly the wscript-hop blind spot both reviewers had flagged). Researched the AV heuristic (sourced): two independent triggers — (1) `-EncodedCommand`+`-ExecutionPolicy Bypass` together, (2) the `wscript→powershell` process tree — the encoding adopted to pass code review is itself a top AV flag; the flash and AV-safety are the same property (visibility). **Reverted** to the git-tracked durable prior version (`notes/gen382/…`, hash-verified byte-identical) through a second `/vet-code` mint/apply — backup works, AV quiet, flash returns. Recommended (via `/suggest`): **accept the flash** (cosmetic; chasing it already broke a working backup once), with the Scheduled-Task approach as the proper AV-safe future option. **Reconciliation with the concurrent `(3)` session (IMPORTANT — surfaced for Erez):** that session, running against my *installed* VBS version, diagnosed the same `BACKUP-SPAWN-*.VBS` popup as a false positive on legit tooling and filed **GEN-433** (Erez-assigned) to add an Avast `...\Temp\backup-spawn-*.vbs` exception. **After THIS session's revert, the hook generates NO `.vbs` at all → GEN-433's premise is moot** (excluding a file that no longer exists). GEN-433 should be closed/repurposed — flagged for Erez, not unilaterally closed (another session's Erez-assigned ticket). - 2026-07-13 (4) — **[GEN-394](https://app.notion.com/p/3986e495d07c81398185ccb116b4ab06) → Done: diagnosed the auto-mode permission classifier's "temporarily unavailable" block, and in doing so OVERTURNED the ticket's own premise.** Erez opened with just "gen 394." The ticket had concluded the message was a *disguised classifier denial* (inferring this from "bypass-permissions mode fixed it"). That inference is invalid — **bypass mode skips the classifier entirely**, so it resolves the symptom whether the classifier was *denying* or *unreachable*; it can't discriminate. **True cause:** a genuine transient availability/overload failure of Claude Code's *separate, server-configured* classifier model (independent of `/model`); auto mode **fails closed by design** when the classifier can't decide. Answered all four open questions: (1) real transient failure, not disguised denial; (2) plain calls trip it because any non-working-dir/non-read-only action routes through the classifier and it was down — the blocked `tail` was a *compound* command (`|| echo`), so not treated as a bare read; (3) **distinct** from GEN-316 (that's the classifier *denying* a sanctioned call; this is the classifier being *unreachable*); (4) no user-configurable fix — mitigations are retry-after-seconds (auto-retries) or read-only work; auto-pauses to manual prompts after 3-in-a-row/20-total. Verified against official Claude Code docs ([permission-modes](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permission-modes), [errors](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/errors)) AND Anthropic's own issue tracker ([#68437](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/68437), [#49535](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/49535), [#74949](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/74949) — the compound-command finding; [#69726](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/69726) — a **persistent multi-day variant** where retry won't help, so the disposition flags that case for an Anthropic `/feedback` report). Diagnosis appended to the ticket (original problem statement preserved), status → In Progress → **Done** (both set directly on the primary ticket, verified by REST re-fetch). **`/check`-converged in 1 round** (pre-mortem/premise-challenge, holistic, soundness+grounding — all PASS, no material findings); the panel's evidence-framing corrections (a tautological log statistic and an over-strong "always transient" claim in my first draft) were incorporated before finalizing. That reasoning miss → **GEN-58 Class-D new element** [GEN-394 tautological-evidence-as-proof] (Vol. 4, count 6→7; index digest added; flagged as a classification judgment call — arguably its own class). The GEN-394 symptom recurred live during this very wrap (classifier overloaded); worked around it per the diagnosis (retry / avoid compound shell commands). **Post-wrap re-evaluation (Erez prompt: "it repeated during the wrap — re-check the conclusion"):** pulled this session's actual denial-log entries and found a FACTUAL ERROR in my own diagnosis — I'd claimed these "temporarily unavailable" events are NOT captured in `credential-denials.jsonl`; they ARE (all 8 session blocks logged by the `PermissionDenied` hook, exactly like true denials). Corrected the ticket (verified) and noted the log can't distinguish transient-unavailable from genuine-denial by itself (no reason-category recorded) — only an identical retry succeeding/failing tells them apart. **Core conclusion HELD and was strengthened:** every one of the 8 blocked calls succeeded on identical retry within seconds — a denial (a decision) would be deterministic, so fail-then-succeed proves availability failure, not denial; the persistence was just a longer overload window, and even bare `git add`/`git push` got caught (refines the earlier compound-command emphasis). Logged the factual miss as a **GEN-58 Class-D recurrence** [GEN-394 claimed-log-contents-without-reading] (Vol. 4, 51x→52x) — distinct from the (4)-entry's tautological-evidence element. Unresolved items filed: none.

## 2026-07-20 (8) — Three-field priority system designed for Team-Tasks (Importance + Gain ratio + derived Priority)
<!-- session:a17f632c-3e7c-475c-b1cb-0b9c030e41d7 -->

**Request:** "From now on, every time you file a Notion ticket, file it with a priority" — plus suggest how, and a priority system. Evolved through pushback into a full three-field data-model design.

**What was designed (not yet built — awaiting Erez's OK-to-build next session):**
- **Importance** (select): Urgent / Not-urgent / Nice-to-have.
- **Gain ratio** (select): 1 (best payoff — big gain for little effort) / 2 / 3 (worst). Confirmed 1 = best.
- **Priority** (select, set by hand): Highest / High / Medium / Low — derived from a 9-cell Importance × Gain table (monotonic; "Highest" only for Urgent + Gain 1; Urgent never < High; Nice-to-have never > Medium). Manual, not a formula field, because a Notion formula outputs plain text, not a coloured/groupable select.

**How the design converged:** started as a single-field urgency scale → Erez added gain÷effort → /check panel killed the literal division as "fake precision" (rewrote as plain Steal/Middling/Trap verdicts) → Erez restructured into three separate fields with a derived Priority → verified formula-field constraint → landed on manual select + 9-cell table. Each stage ran through /suggest + /check (all converged, mostly ≤2 rounds).

**Tickets filed:**
- **GEN-512** (Medium) — Build three-field priority model in Team-Tasks. Self-contained build spec: field definitions, 9-cell table, data-source ID, MCP DDL how-to, the one open build decision (repurpose existing Priority field vs. build fresh), acceptance criteria. Parented to GEN-86.
- **GEN-513** (Low) — Encode the three-field priority system as a global CLAUDE.md rule. Depends on GEN-512; must go through /vet-rule; flags the redundancy risk vs. the existing "draft the full proposal … priority …" rule (likely extend it, not add a new rule). Parented to GEN-86, cross-linked to GEN-512.

**Fixed during wrap-verify:** GEN-512's "Parent item" had been set to GEN-513 by the sibling-relation write (single-value relation collision); corrected back to the GEN-86 epic and re-verified.

**Unresolved items filed: none** (both follow-ups GEN-512/513 filed mid-session; no new items surfaced at wrap).

**Reversals judged: 1 learning, 1 non-learning.**
- Learning (logged to GEN-58, Class D recurrence of the GEN-444 element, seen 2x): leaned "auto-computed Priority" as a pickable option before verifying a Notion formula can't output a coloured select — Erez's "Are you sure?" surfaced it; capability asserted before verification.
- Non-learning: two exhaustiveness overstatements ("the only downside", "the only thing left") were caught by the claim-linter hook in-turn and corrected before reaching a decision — the wired trigger already enforces it, so no new rule/ticket.

## 2026-07-20 (6) — /wrap learning-capture: reversal-capture rule + near-duplicate dedupe
<!-- session:ef5b356b-15d1-4b39-892f-67b563d5b8f1 -->

**Two vetted additions to the `/wrap` skill (Step 1), both applied via `/check` → `/vet-rule`:**

1. **Reversal-capture as a learning source.** At `/wrap`, every time Erez's pushback made Claude change an answer it had already given is now a behavioral-learning candidate — whether he challenged a position (Claude reversed) or asked a probing question whose honest answer exposed a gap Claude should have caught in its own `/suggest`/`/check` pass. Claude judges whether the flip reveals a reasoning/process weakness; if so → file a learning ticket, if not → one-line "Reversals judged non-learning:" note in HISTORY so the negative judgments stay auditable. Independent of GEN-58 (the immediate in-session log); a reversal may qualify for both. Widened from the initial narrow "reversal only" scope after Erez asked whether it would catch the probing-question case (it wouldn't have — a live instance of the very gap the rule now covers). `/check` converged in 3 rounds.

2. **Near-duplicate dedupe (new Step 1 sub-step 6).** Before filing a new learning ticket, semantic-search open Team-Tasks tickets for one about the same underlying issue (the existing sub-step 5 catches exact-title only). On a confident same-behavior/same-root-cause match → merge this session's note into that open ticket (`insert_content`, dated "Also surfaced:" line) instead of creating a duplicate; on any doubt → file new. Open tickets only (post-filter drops Done/Review/closed, since `notion-search` can't filter by status; also excludes same-run IDs). Every merge surfaces in the Step 7 report. `/check` converged in 2 rounds.

**Decisions (Erez):** capture *every* reversal (not only flaw-revealing ones), Claude self-judges which possess a learning; judgment at `/wrap` (not mid-session); merge-when-confident/file-when-unsure; open tickets only; widen the reversal trigger (vs. a separate trigger) to cover probing questions.

**This session's learnings (dogfooding the new rules at this very wrap):**
- Silence-on-success violated again (narrated a clean self-correction; Erez pushed back "why are you telling me all this") → **merged** into GEN "Enforce the silence-on-success self-audit narration ban automatically" (same behavior + root cause).
- Probing-question self-review gap ("would this catch X?" → "no") → already resolved this session (the reversal rule is the fix); GEN-58 is its standing home. Not filed.
- **GEN-511 (new):** the `/vet-rule`/`/vet-code` single-use check pass can't cover a multi-edit change — the first edit consumed the pass and left `SKILL.md` half-applied, and the revert was also gated. Recovery: re-mint + single full-file Write. Candidate fix: `/vet-rule` Step 5 should apply the full end-state as one write.

**Process notes:** both edits' installed files hash-verified against the `/check`-approved text. The near-duplicate edit hit the half-applied-state snag (GEN-511) mid-apply; resolved by re-mint + one full-file Write, final hash matched.

## 2026-07-20 (7): GEN-501 signal-surfacing enforcer shipped (two hooks + /wrap Step 3b)
<!-- session:38697241-76cc-4c83-92d5-ee55cbbbf46d -->

Built, vetted, installed, and live-verified GEN-501 — the strong (automatic) enforcer for GEN-484's signal-surfacing rule. Erez's initial ask was an effort estimate; escalated to a full build on his go-ahead.

**What shipped (all live + backed up):**
- `hooks/posttooluse-signal-detect.js` — PostToolUse, matcher `Write|Edit|MultiEdit|NotebookEdit`. Scans written content/path for signal-producing patterns (appendFileSync/counter/metric/increment; fresh `.jsonl`/`.log` path on a Write). On match: appends a `detect` line to durable `hooks/signal-surface-pending.jsonl` THEN writes an OS-temp per-turn marker (detect-before-marker write order enforces the no-orphan-deliver invariant). Emits no stdout; fail-open; watchdog.
- `hooks/stop-signal-surface.js` — Stop hook (4th). Consumes the marker, appends a paired `deliver` line, and if the finished message shows no surfacing-design language (co-occurrence check: a surfacing-intent word within 160 chars of a signal-context word or the triggering file's basename) injects a non-blocking nudge embedding the GEN-467 no-block clause.
- `/wrap` Step 3b — reports unpaired (crash-orphaned) detects to the For-you block; report-only + append-only (no compaction — the GEN-218 unlocked-rewrite hazard was designed out after the panel flagged it).
- Registered both in `settings.json`; added both to `sync.ps1` FileMap (durable log covered by existing `*.jsonl` ignore).

**Gate path:** design `/check` (2 rounds, converged) → impl `/check` on the code (2 rounds, converged) → `/vet-code` Step 3 two code-review passes (in-session high + independent Opus; caught + fixed the MultiEdit/NotebookEdit coverage gap and tightened the false-clear heuristic) → non-trivial-fix re-review PASS → live-verify 34/34 fixture assertions via real node-invocation → installed byte-identical → live-fired end-to-end at installed paths (detector wrote detect, deliverer paired + nudged correctly). Step 4a (antivirus) N/A — no background launch.

**Key design facts established (from primary sources this session):** GEN-484 was literally a single `.jsonl`-append Write, so the Write-content detector catches its own seed incident. Stop payload has NO `tool_calls` but HAS `transcript_path` (confirmed in `gen450-stop-probe.jsonl`) — justifies the two-hook split over a transcript-parsing single hook (transcript lags async writes). PostToolUse fires main-session-only → sub-agent-built signals are an accepted gap (global rule is backstop), alongside shell-written and non-file signals.

**Reasoning correction worth noting:** my first `/wrap`-edit draft added a 30-day log-compaction step; the panel showed it (a) silently deleted the very un-surfaced signals the log exists to preserve and (b) re-triggered the detector on its own temp-file rewrite. Removed entirely — append-only, matching the accepted `credential-denials.jsonl` precedent.

GEN-501 → Done (verified live, no pending re-check). Parent GEN-484 fully complete.

Unresolved items filed: GEN-510 (observe & tune the detector's false-nudge rate — code-review advisory, unassigned, under GEN-86).

## 2026-07-20 (5) — GEN-495 shipped: mechanical-lane hardening (in-fence gate + defer-not-silent-approve)
<!-- session:3f1294e4-4de3-4a01-ad17-b568ddce3184 -->

**GEN-495 → Done.** Hardened the check-before-present gate's mechanical-fix lane in `~/.claude/hooks/auto-approve.js`, via full `/vet-code`.

**Two behavioral fixes (one coherent change):**
1. **In-fence residual closed.** A whitespace-only Edit whose fragment sat wholly inside a fenced code block (no ``` in the fragment) previously classified "mechanical" and was auto-approved without `/check`. Fix: a 3-line `fileHasFence(key)` helper — if a check-due file contains ``` anywhere, an Edit/MultiEdit to it is non-mechanical. Mirrors the Write path's existing whole-file stance; fails safe on read error.
2. **Mechanical lane no longer silently approves.** `enforceCheckDue`'s mechanical-only branch changed from `approve()` (silent `permissionDecision:'allow'`) to `defer()` — a genuine whitespace-only fix now falls through to Erez's normal confirmation prompt, matching the always-loaded CLAUDE.md rule ("mechanical fix needs confirmation but not a /check"), which the old silent-approve contradicted. Non-mechanical (incl. in-fence) still hard-blocks.
- Also updated `~/.claude/skills/vet-rule/SKILL.md`'s stale "Mechanical-fix exemption" bullet to match. Global CLAUDE.md rule left unchanged (re-read, confirmed already accurate).

**Design evolution:** first proposed dropping the lane entirely ("strictly safer, removes code"); the design `/check` panel caught this collides with the always-loaded rule (Erez chose to reconcile: defer, not drop). Then proposed a fence-offset position parser (`fragmentInsideFence`, ~35 lines); round-1 `/check` (holistic lens) found it disproportionate/fragile for a locked security file → simplified to the whole-file `fileHasFence`.

**Vetting (full /vet-code):** design /check converged (2 rounds); two code-review passes (Pass A in-session Opus 4.8, Pass B independent cold Opus sub-agent) both clean; `node --check` passes; 7-case live-verify through the real hook ALL PASS, incl. a before/after contrast proving the live pre-change hook silently approved the in-fence case and the fixed hook blocks it. Installed files byte-identical to approved copies; installed hook live-fired ALL PASS. No background launch → Step 4a (antivirus) N/A → verified this session.

**Reasoning miss logged:** GEN-58 Class D (new element — "governing-rule-not-consulted-for-mechanism-change": recommended the fix as self-contained "strictly safer" before /check caught the always-loaded-rule collision; classification judgment call flagged to Erez).

## 2026-07-20 (5) — Ticket-completeness bar added to the global ticket-drafting rule
<!-- session:00aa7395-bfef-4f57-8701-de4d229807eb -->

**Problem Erez raised:** tickets Claude creates often lack enough context to act on later, so he repeatedly has to ask "does it have all needed context? verify" and gets told "no." He wanted the ticket born complete.

**What shipped:** extended the always-loaded global `CLAUDE.md` ticket-drafting rule ("Before creating or materially changing a ticket in a tracker, draft the full proposal…") with a completeness bar: the drafted body must stand on its own — problem+why, background to act (paths/IDs/links/prior decisions), next action or acceptance criteria, who verifies it where relevant — re-read against that bar before presenting; scaled to complexity; with an inline "not 'unrequested info'" guard and a descriptive carve-out for the reasoning-failure log-writes exception. Applied via `update-global-rule.ps1` through the `/vet-rule` flow (check-record + minted check pass), exit 0, verified live and synced to Drive.

**How it got there (the reasoning arc worth keeping):**
1. First proposal — put the bar in the `notion-howto` skill — was **killed by the /check panel**: all three reviewers + this repo's own HISTORY (2026-07-01) confirmed skills do NOT auto-load, so a skill-homed rule fires only when explicitly invoked = the exact unreliability being fixed. Claude had asserted "notion-howto already loads at that moment" from the skill's description without verifying — a verify-before-asserting miss Erez's rule already covers.
2. Reframed to a resident rule; Erez's question "don't we already have a rule?" surfaced that the fix belonged folded into the existing line-59 rule (not a new standalone rule), since line 59 already fires at draft time and governs the body.
3. Contradiction check (prompted by Erez) caught a real conflict: the bar, nested in line 59, could be read as NOT covered by the GEN-58 immediate-log exception (line 185) → would force immediate reasoning-failure logs to satisfy a stand-alone bar. Fixed with an explicit carve-out.
4. Minimality pass (prompted by Erez "is this the shortest?") trimmed a full hedge sentence to a ~5-word inline tag; split reviewer verdict resolved on the shared cheap fix.
5. Final vet-rule confirming pass caught that the carve-out hardcoded "GEN-58" (a ticket number) into an always-loaded file — a line-155 violation — fixed by referencing the exception descriptively instead.
6. Confirmed (Erez's questions) the mechanical/behavioral split: behavioral ticket rules stay always-loaded; Notion tool *gotchas* already live in the hook-injected `hooks/refs/notion.md`, not the skill. Nothing moved.

**Follow-up filed:** GEN-508 — "Add a structural ticket-creation gate enforcing the completeness bar" (GEN-86, Backlog, task, unassigned): optional hard-gate hardening of the behavioral rule, build only if the rule proves insufficient over the next several tickets.

Unresolved items filed: [GEN-508]

Dropped learnings: the "verify skill auto-load before relying on it" lesson — already covered by the global "Verify before asserting" rule and already recorded in the 2026-07-01 HISTORY entry; not re-filed.

## 2026-07-20 (5) — Silence-on-success self-audit narration ban (global CLAUDE.md)
<!-- session:1479d958-a207-433e-97e5-66e7f895b6c1 -->

**Trigger.** Erez asked why, in another session ("Opus 4.8 effort for gen 501"), a reply ended with a self-audit line: "…no unverified assertions. No state was changed. No block is owed." — and to verify it.

**Investigation.** Located and re-read that session's transcript (via session-mgmt tools). Verdict: the three claims in that line were factually true and read-only, but writing the line at all was a mistake — that turn was a pure Q&A owing no "📌 For you" block, so under "Default to silence on success" it owed silence, not a narrated self-audit. Correctly concluding "no block is owed" means emitting nothing, not a sentence saying so.

**Prior coverage.** Searched under GEN-86: the issue lineage already exists — GEN-151 (original report of an unneeded process note, merged), GEN-314 ("Stop surfacing for-me-only content", Done — added the governing rule). The rule existed; the failure was non-application, plus a narrow gap (a clean/null verdict reading as a "conclusion" rather than "the checking").

**Decision & fix.** Erez chose to fix via a rule edit (not a hook), broad "Scope 2" (never emit any commentary about internal process on a silence-owed turn). Formed the edit via /suggest; ran /check to convergence — 3 rounds, 4 lenses (pre-mortem, holistic, soundness, rule-check; final round on Opus). R1: all REVISE (edit anchored to narrow verification clause; false premise that the opening sentence didn't already cover it; over-fit enumeration). R2: soundness+pre-mortem PASS, holistic+rule-check REVISE (closed list still echoed the incident phrase; NEW conflict — absolute "never as a free-standing note" contradicted the unchanged "a claim you must flag as unverified" carve-out; clarity risk). R3: both PASS — incident echo dropped, true catch-all, conflict fixed via "never overrides the exception stream below or a report a standing rule requires inside the block", reasoning-as-content carve-out added.

**Applied** via /vet-rule gate: wrote check-record (referencing real R3 reviewer transcripts), minted single-use check pass (Erez approved), applied through update-global-rule.ps1 (exit 0, synced to Drive), verified installed file hash matches approved working copy byte-for-byte. The edit adds a clause to the "Default to silence on success" rule (global CLAUDE.md line 73).

**Advisory carried forward.** Reviewers judged the root cause was likely non-application of an already-covering rule, so text alone may not fully prevent recurrence; Erez knowingly chose rule-over-hook. Enforcement is the deferred lever → filed GEN-507 with a re-evaluate bar (build only if the failure recurs).

**Tickets:** GEN-507 created (enforcement follow-up, Backlog, unassigned, under GEN-86).
Unresolved items filed: GEN-507
Dropped learnings: none

## 2026-07-20 (5): GEN-503 shipped — vetting-gate fail-open fixed via /vet-code; wider settings.json blast radius found; GEN-504/505/506 filed
<!-- session:ae834e73-22a8-42a8-9840-dc3e51c8e746 -->

**GEN-503 — vetting-gate fail-open (Done).** Fixed the fail-open in `auto-approve.js`'s vetting gate: `resolveUpdateConfigFile`'s `-File` extraction regex `/-File\s+.../i` was unanchored, so for `-Op write-file -File auto-approve.js` it matched the `-file` TAIL of the token `write-file`, captured the next token `-File` as the "filename", the `$ManagedFiles` lookup failed → null → no vetting target → gate deferred (fail-open). Net effect: EVERY `-Op write-file` update-config.ps1 call bypassed the vetting-pass requirement. Fix = prepend a non-capturing leading boundary `(?:^|\s)` to the flag match in `resolveUpdateConfigFile` (`-File`) and `ucArg` (`-Op`/`-ContentFile`). Two functional lines; the rest comments (incl. tightening three misleading "fails safe → prompt" comments). Installed byte-identical (normalized sha256 `9e66b18d…50eda2`).

**Wider blast radius than the ticket documented:** because the buggy resolve returned null for every write-file, the global `settings.json` **hooks-registration arm** (`settingsHooksChanged` via `k === GLOBAL_SETTINGS_KEY`) was ALSO failing open for write-file edits — not just hook/script `.js`/`.ps1` writes. The fix restores that arm too. Surfaced by the /check pre-mortem reviewer.

## 2026-07-19 (5) — GEN-469 investigated: step-1 note reframe shipped, step-2 logger rejected by review and parked
<!-- session:cb50db9e-3af7-4117-9ae9-2792f78fb999 -->

**Task:** Investigate the latest occurrence of GEN-469 (model narrating end-of-turn Stop-hook nudges back to Erez instead of acting on them silently) and suggest a fix.

**Investigation.** GEN-469 was marked Done (2026-07-17) after two instruction-based fixes (reworded claim-linter nudge + a "Default to silence on success" CLAUDE.md clause), but recurred repeatedly on 2026-07-19 — both mechanisms live and correct, behavior still happened for both the claim-linter nudge AND the GEN-422 For-you nudge. Diagnosed as an ADHERENCE failure, not a missing-instruction gap. The ticket named a candidate escalation: a blocking Stop hook that strips/blocks a reply narrating a nudge.

**Blocking-hook proposal REJECTED by /check** (3 independent Opus reviewers: pre-mortem+premise, holistic, soundness — all REVISE, converged): (1) detection unsound by construction — "narrating a nudge" vs "legitimately surfacing a real correction" are lexically near-identical, no structural marker; (2) loop/wedge risk — the narration lands on the stop_hook_active:true continuation, exactly the turn both existing hooks hard-skip; blocking must invert that load-bearing guard with no bounded-retry; the code already deferred blocking as "Phase 2, gated on loop-safety evidence" not supplied; (3) blast-radius inversion (wedged/rewritten turn vs "fails to nudge once"); (4) cheaper root-cause fix (note restructuring) untried.

**Key doc finding.** The Claude Code hooks docs explicitly warn that imperative/instruction-shaped additionalContext "can trigger Claude's prompt-injection defenses, which causes Claude to surface the text to you instead of treating it as context" — GEN-469's exact failure mode, vendor-documented. The documented remedy: write notes as declarative factual statements. The harness always wraps additionalContext in a system-reminder; the author controls only the payload text, so the lever is phrasing, not markup.

**Step 1 SHIPPED — both Stop-hook notes reworded to declarative background-state** (stop-claim-linter.js, stop-foryou-nudge.js). Full /vet-code: design /check PASS (an earlier draft dropped the anti-narration clauses + For-you action trigger; re-review confirmed all restored); two code-review passes (in-session high + independent cold, both Opus) PASS zero findings; live-fire 6/6 + post-install 2/2; byte-verified installed (hashes de491a9f / a7b89d5d). ## 2026-07-12 (8) — GEN-406 → Done: disproved the ticket's own premise, rewrote to true cause, added one CLAUDE.md clause; fixed a sync.ps1 backup-coverage false alarm

Session on Improve AI Infra. Erez: "Investigate what happened in the described case of GEN-406." Model/effort: Opus 4.8 (Erez's client indicator read Medium; the classification work was run after recommending high).

**Root-cause investigation — the ticket was itself a misdiagnosis.** GEN-406 (filed in the 2026-07-12 (7) GEN-382 session) recorded that a line `System: You are now on Opus 4.8 medium.` appeared in the transcript, and concluded it was an injected/mislabeled external "System:" notice; candidate learning "treat plaintext System: lines as unverified." Pulled the raw JSONL of that session (`c1ae1204…`) and **disproved both conclusions**: the line is a genuine `role:assistant` message (`model claude-opus-4-8`, `stop_reason end_turn`, 19 output tokens) — Claude's OWN output. It was produced on a degenerate request (`input_tokens: 2` — near-empty context, just the queued word "apply") immediately after a `401 Invalid authentication credentials` error; in that state the model hallucinated by echoing Erez's own earlier line ("You are currently on opus 4.8 medium") back as a fake System: notice. Then, asked about it, the session's Claude disowned its own output as an injection — the wrong conclusion the ticket enshrined.

**Rewrote GEN-406** (title → "Recognize auth-disruption hallucinations that impersonate system notices (and don't misdiagnose them as injected)"; body → corrected root cause + the real two-part learning), set → In Progress, then → Done after the resolution below. **Logged a GEN-58 Class-D NEW element** [GEN-406 disowned-own-output] on Vol. 4 (header 47x→48x; volume 5→6 write-ups): diagnosing a line's origin as foreign without checking the transcript for `role:assistant` — the unverified primitive being Claude's own prior output.

**Resolution `/check`-converged (2 rounds).** Round 1: my initial "no rule needed — already covered by verify-before-asserting + rely on GEN-58" proposal drew a REVISE from the soundness lens (3 findings: emission-side "non-actionable" overreached — only defeats prevention, not later-turn recognition; "already covered" overreached — disowning own output is a distinct authorship-misattribution failure, not stale recall; "infeasible" overreached — grep shows not-currently-built). Revised: the two learnings collapse to one; appended ONE clause to the existing global `CLAUDE.md` "Verify before asserting" rule (line 39) naming the authorship/origin-misattribution case; no hook (can't judge "looks foreign"), no skill (no named trigger). Round 2: soundness re-review all-RESOLVED + fresh rule-check PASS. **Applied** via `update-global-rule.ps1` (exit 0, synced); verified the clause present in the live file and byte-identical local↔Drive (987 chars each). Caveat recorded on the ticket: failure observed exactly once; "will recur" is reasoned, not empirical.

**Backup-coverage false alarm fixed.** The `sync.ps1` run surfaced a recurring `BACKUP-COVERAGE` warning about a top-level `tasks/` folder. Verified it is the harness's ephemeral per-session scratch task-list store (session-UUID-keyed, holds a `.lock` + numbered `.json`) — same class as `sessions`/`session-env`, which are already ignored; it should NOT be backed up. Added `'tasks'` to `$IgnoreDirs` in `Test-BackupCoverage` (the watchdog's own "declare per-machine" remedy). Applied directly (`sync.ps1` is not a locked file, one-token data-array change) and live-verified: re-ran sync, the `tasks` warning is gone and no new warning appeared.

## 2026-07-12 (2) — GEN-393 SOLVED & APPLIED: learning triage built into `/wrap` Step 1 (v5.2); backlog sweep deferred

## 2026-07-12 (1) — GEN-382: design converged further (multi-session/multi-PC/other-tools + option c ruled out); scope narrowed; both decisions CLOSED late-session

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Fable 5 then Opus 4.8. Continuation of 2026-07-09 (4). Erez asked to take the design work on GEN-382 further, ask clarifying questions, and return a converged plan. **Nothing was built or applied** — still investigation + design. Mutations this session: GEN-382 (a second handoff section appended, after the 07-09 one — both intact, REST-verified) and two GEN-58 log writes (one Class-C recurrence + index bump). The five locked decisions and the (a)/(b)/(c) framing from 07-09 are unchanged; this session refined (b) and added a sub-choice.

**Option (c) surfaced by Erez's pushback and ruled out.** Erez asked whether a local folder on `C:` mirrored by Google Drive's desktop client ("Folders from your computer") shares (b)'s downsides. Verified (WebSearch + `/check`, 2 rounds): real (installed client v127 supports it) but NOT airtight — documented `.git` corruption from Drive writing into synced folders (desktop.ini injection), and **the client cannot exclude `.git`** (verified, no such feature); deletions propagate (mirror, not history; ~30-day trash); no secret gate; silent-pause risk. If a Drive copy is ever wanted, the safe route is Drive as an *additional destination of (b)* (push to an archive repo inside the synced folder), itself unproven against the corruption class → needs a live test. My presenting the choice as a closed (a)/(b) binary was the Class-C reasoning miss (see below).

**(b) stress-tested — all `/check`-converged (3 lenses, 2 rounds each):**
- Same-PC concurrency (4-5 sessions/day on one repo is current reality): collisions → retry → snapshot delayed minutes, never corrupts; files always safe on disk. Mitigations are TO-BE-BUILT (not true today): isolated-index snapshots + **per-session** ref naming (finer than per-machine — the soundness lens caught that per-machine alone still races same-PC sessions).
- Separate backup channel (refs outside `refs/heads/*`) keeps main history curated; those refs are invisible in GitHub's web UI but restorable ("not on GitHub.com" ≠ "not backed up").
- Hard rules the machinery must obey: reads+pushes only (no pull/rebase/checkout/reset); every snapshot pushed to a NAMED ref (dangling commits get GC'd ~14 days); detect + loudly flag a mid-merge repo rather than snapshot conflict debris.
- Multi-PC (future): per-machine + per-session ref namespacing; git refuses silent overwrite of diverged work (verified); Drive-mirror instead makes conflict-copies. Costs: per-PC one-script install; the catch-all repo needs per-machine labeling for "promote?" triage.
- Cursor/other tools = biggest limit of hook-only (b): a PostToolUse hook only fires on *Claude* writes. Fix = a **scheduled sweep** (Windows task ~15-30 min, tool-agnostic). Three sweep caveats each got a design answer to live-test at build: sleep gap → catch-up-on-wake + dead-man's-switch; torn mid-save files → settle-time (snapshot only files quiet ~1 min) + never overwrite older snapshots; scheduled-task GitHub credentials → live-test.
- Inherent git limits recorded: ignored files, quarantined secrets, >100 MB (future game/asset project → LFS), snapshot buildup (retention/compaction from day one), network (retried), single-account risk (Drive second target).

**Scope narrowed by Erez this session:** currently one device, Claude-only. Cursor-coverage and multi-device move to **two future tickets Erez already holds — he has not yet given the IDs; next session must ask** and copy the deferred sweep-for-other-tools + multi-PC analysis onto them.

**Sub-choice within (b): Claude's converged lean = SWEEP-ONLY FIRST.** On the scope note I first flipped the lean to hook-first (reasoning: Claude-only = nothing changes while Claude is closed); a `/check` pass caught this as an overcorrection (build-complexity favors the sweep — one scheduled task vs. building concurrent-commit collision-safety; and non-Claude writes like hand-edits/downloads still happen on one device, so the sweep's catch-all coverage still matters) and I reverted. Sweep-only = one task, single actor, no collision machinery, covers non-Claude changes in minutes, is the backbone the future tickets need; hook is addable later (an addition, not a redesign) if the ~15-30 min lag ever matters. Key distinction surfaced for Erez: next-session latency for non-Claude edits means *no backup exists at all* in that window — unlike the accepted transient-failure case (already backed up, only a push retry pending).

**Two decisions remain OPEN (handed off on GEN-382):** (1) top-level (a) relocate-to-Drive / (b) git-backup / (c) Drive-mirror — my rec (b); (2) if (b), sweep-only vs hook+sweep — my lean sweep-only. Plus: Erez to name the two future tickets. Next session: once decided, build → `/check` the concrete design → `/code-review` any hook/script before applying.

**GEN-58 (Class C — premature narrow-options claim) recurrence, 16x (last 2026-07-12):** presented the top-level backup direction as a closed (a)/(b) binary; Erez's "doesn't a local folder synced to Drive…?" surfaced the never-enumerated (c). Real option, ultimately flawed; caught by pushback pre-decision, no damage. Noted: the binary framing had itself survived the prior day's `/check` panel — the holistic lens reviewed the *design*, not the option-set completeness. Wrote the trace on Vol. 4 + bumped the index (15x→16x), both REST-verified.

**Late-session: compact-gate bug found + filed → [GEN-398](https://app.notion.com/p/39b6e495d07c81f4950dda12de0e805d) (Bug, To Do, under GEN-86, unassigned).** After the Notion+HISTORY logging above, `/compact` was blocked by `precompact-loggate.js` even though the session was freshly logged. Diagnosed from source (read both files): the gate flag is WRITTEN by `/loghistory` step 4 as `touch .loggate-ok-$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` (shell env var) but READ by the hook via `data.session_id` (hook stdin) — if the two ids diverge the flag is invisible to the gate. Compounding: `$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` was observed non-empty at the first `/loghistory` this session ("GATE SET: 81e18f00…") but EMPTY on a later live check — so the var populates at session start and is lost after the first `/compact`. Unexplained (left for research on GEN-398): the `81e18f00` flag reported set at session start is absent from `~/.claude/`, and no empty-suffix flag exists either. Corrected my own first wrong explanation mid-turn (I'd claimed a content-hash gate that re-arms per compact; reading `precompact-loggate.js` showed it's a plain per-session flag-file check, no hashing). GEN-398 filed via REST; verified new ticket props + that GEN-86 was NOT re-parented (destructive-op (c) check clean). **Deliberately-not-done:** no fix attempted — filed for a future research session. Nothing built/applied this session; the only project-file mutations were HISTORY.md edits.

Erez then reshaped Decision 2 across three questions (each answer `/check`-converged): trigger on `/wrap`+`/loghistory`? → yes, in addition, fire-and-forget; trigger at session start? → yes, and no session-tracking needed (the sweep backs up whatever changed since last run); can those replace the timer? → laid out the exposure honestly (backup only protects against disasters *after* it runs). **Final: Decision 1 = GitHub backup home; Decision 2 = NO scheduled timer** — one sweep script fired by (a) startup/logon, (b) Claude session start (incl. resume/post-compact), (c) `/wrap` and `/loghistory`; all fire-and-forget with a single-instance lock (skip when busy). **Erez explicitly accepted the residual exposure** (work unprotected until the next trigger — hours mid-session, days if the machine stays on without Claude) as non-material for now; a ~20-min timer stays a documented add-on. Recorded on GEN-382 as "DECISIONS CLOSED — 2026-07-12" (REST/MCP verified; the old "TWO OPEN DECISIONS" header marked superseded). Model guidance for next steps: build = Fable 5 high (Opus 4.8 acceptable for mechanical phases); finding the two future tickets = Sonnet 5 low. Also: evidence comment added to GEN-398 (`$CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` empty in the PowerShell tool but populated in the Bash tool, same session — shell-specific, narrows the research). ## 2026-07-09 (4) — GEN-382 renamed to "Make backup of everything we work on airtight" → In Progress; first design failed `/check`; pivot designed; decision handed off

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Opus 4.8. Erez asked to go over GEN-382 and return with guiding questions or a converged plan. **Nothing was built or applied** — this was investigation + design that ended on an open decision, fully handed off onto the ticket. The only mutations: GEN-382 (handoff section + status + rename) and two GEN-58 log writes.

**What GEN-382 was, and what it became.** Filed as "Backup Gap" — one hook file (`notion-fetch-staleness.js`) flagged as missing from `sync.ps1`'s backup set. Investigation found that file is *already* backed up in all three locations and already in `$FileMap` (sync.ps1:136) — **but Erez had added it manually** at a past `/wrap`. That manual fix IS the point: the existing coverage watchdog (`Test-BackupCoverage`, built under GEN-140, warn-only) is not airtight — it emits a warning nobody is forced to act on. Erez restated the real goal: **every file we create or change, across all current and future projects, backed up by a mechanism that can't be silently skipped** — not just config. Ticket renamed accordingly (verified via REST) and set → In Progress (primary ticket, substantive work).

**Also resolved the ticket's "covered elsewhere?" ask:** GEN-382 is essentially a duplicate of [GEN-140](https://app.notion.com/p/3746e495d07c81bab600ce35574d8498) (Done — built the coverage watchdog). GEN-382 now carries the broader airtight goal GEN-140 didn't.

**5 design decisions Erez locked (via one-at-a-time Q&A):** (1) scope = everything, config is just one category; (2) if a file isn't backed up, back it up automatically — no prompt/block — EXCEPT a suspected secret, which surfaces (fail-closed scan before anything leaves the machine); (3) transient push failure (network/GitHub/Drive down) never blocks — wrap completes, unmissable notice, next session retries; (4) enumeration = session-driven minus provably-throwaway (scratchpad/`%TEMP%`/transcripts, `node_modules`/build), uncertainty always resolves to backing up; (5) brand-new location → protect in a catch-all + flag "promote to its own repo?", never auto-create a named GitHub repo.

**The first design FAILED `/check` (3 reviewers — pre-mortem, holistic, soundness — all REVISE).** Design was "enumerate changed files at session end via the two edit logs, then back them up." Two premises were **verified false from source** (reviewers read the files):
- **Edit logs are incomplete** (`auto-approve.js`): `auto-approved-edits.jsonl` logs only Edit/Write/MultiEdit into project roots — not NotebookEdit, not shell-classified-safe writes (script output etc.), not moves/renames/deletes; `deferred-calls.jsonl` logs only unsafe/unrecognized calls. So log-based enumeration silently misses whole write classes — the exact failure this ticket exists to kill, moved one layer down.
- **There is no `SessionEnd` event** (`settings.json` registers only UserPromptSubmit/PreToolUse/PostToolUse/PermissionDenied/Stop/SessionStart/PreCompact). `Stop` is per-turn; `/wrap` fires only when Erez types the phrase. So "airtight at session end" = "Erez remembers to type two words" — weaker than the dependency we set out to remove.
- Also flagged: secret scan never wired into the highest-risk (new-location) path; a mid-loop crash of the backup step would report success while skipping files.

**Pivot (design direction, NOT built):** reviewers converged on **continuous backup as work happens** (a PostToolUse hook on writes) **+ a SessionStart catch-up** that reconciles each repo against what's actually backed up using git's own truth (`git status`), not a custom log. Keep the fail-closed secret scan and the no-auto-repo guard.

**Ground truth verified live this session:** all 3 projects are git repos w/ GitHub remotes, clean + pushed, no untracked non-ignored files (only `node_modules` ignored); **project work is backed up ONLY by `git push` at `/wrap`** — NOT in any continuous-sync folder; `C:\Users\Erez\AI Projects` is a plain local dir (not OneDrive, not a link into `G:`); `.claude` is backed up because `sync.ps1` explicitly COPIES it to `G:` + the git-history repo; **git works on the `G:` Drive mount** (init/add/commit/status clean in a temp folder, since deleted) BUT `G:` is a streamed drive flagged risky for live repos; **path-migration cost inventoried** — ~170 files mention the path but nearly all are historical transcripts that don't break; live refs needing edits = global `CLAUDE.md`, `settings.local.json`, `loghistory.md`, `gen292-verify/SKILL.md`, each project's `CLAUDE.md`, Drive-tooling scripts, several of them LOCKED config files.

**OPEN DECISION (Erez said "not sure" — this is the pickup point).** (a) relocate projects to `G:\My Drive\AI Projects` (Erez's initial pick — leans on Drive continuous sync, but live repos on a streamed drive = real reliability risk + locked-file migration) vs. (b) keep projects on `C:` and build the git-based continuous backup + SessionStart catch-up (**Claude's recommendation after verifying the trade-offs** — avoids both the streamed-drive risk and the migration; git+GitHub already gives the offsite continuous backup (a) would). Full handoff (all decisions, findings, the failed design + why, ground truth, the open decision) is appended onto **GEN-382**; the v1 (pre-revision) design spec is in the session scratchpad (`backup-airtight-design.md`).

**Next session:** resolve (a) vs (b) first; then build the chosen mechanism; then re-`/check` the concrete design before applying, and `/vet-code` for any hook/script (guarded infra).

**GEN-58:** logged a Class-D recurrence ("Designed on an unverified primitive," → 46x, dated trace on Vol. 4 + index bump, both verified via REST) — the first design was built on two untested primitives, one of which the artifact itself flagged as unverified yet still went to `/check`. Caught pre-build, no damage.

**Transient note:** the auto-mode classifier was briefly "temporarily unavailable" mid-session (opus-4-8 classifier path), blocking two read-only checks; both succeeded on retry. Not a credential denial, not the GEN-316 condition — handled silently.

## 2026-07-09 (3) — GEN-388: moved to Blocked; classifier "outage" disproved

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Opus 4.8. Erez asked to go over GEN-388 and come back with guiding questions or a converged plan.

**The arc via guiding questions.** Q1 established the trigger is a real-world event, which led Erez to ask whether we have a mechanism that auto-detects the need, updates Notion, and flips Blocked→To Do. We don't (verified — all hooks are injectors/guards/loggers). Erez said he had a ticket for that general mechanism ("b") and proposed: build the general mechanism first, then wire GEN-388 in. **I found the "b" ticket — [GEN-312](https://app.notion.com/p/38d6e495d07c81dcb8ffd3d713554d34) — is already Wont Do** (killed 2026-06-28: after classifying the backlog, the event-driven premise collapsed — most deferred triggers are judgment-based with no catchable signal). GEN-388's trigger ("warnings ignored") is exactly that judgment-based kind, so it can't be auto-detected and GEN-312 wouldn't have covered it. Recommended leaving GEN-388 deferred with GEN-58 recurrence as the wake signal.

**Erez chose to make the deferral explicit.** Moved GEN-388 Backlog→**Blocked**, Reason→**Event Pending** (a select field, only 3 fixed options; the free-text rationale went into a page comment instead of the body, at Erez's suggestion). All property writes verified via live REST. The staleness hook fired mid-session on the GEN-312 read (10-day-stale snapshot) — re-read via REST, which is exactly the hook working as designed.

**The classifier "outage" — disproved.** Across ~4 turns, a Notion `notion-create-comment` write and a read-only `Bash tail` were blocked with "claude-opus-4-8 is temporarily unavailable, so auto mode cannot determine the safety." I diagnosed this as an Opus outage and relayed it as fact repeatedly. **Erez switched the session to bypass-permissions and the identical call succeeded instantly** — proving the blocker was the classifier DECISION PATH, not model unavailability; the "temporarily unavailable" wording is a misleading surface for a classifier-side block. Filed [GEN-394](https://app.notion.com/p/3986e495d07c81398185ccb116b4ab06) (AI-infra) to investigate. Logged the reasoning miss as a **GEN-58 Class-A recurrence** of [watchdog-warning-relayed-as-fact] (trace on Vol. 4; header 23x→24x) — an automated system's message is a hypothesis to verify, not a fact to relay; a cheap discriminating test (toggle mode) existed and I didn't propose it until Erez did.

**GEN-316:** the blocked calls were NOT logged to GEN-316 — that ticket tracks the classifier *misattributing and denying* a sanctioned call; this was a classifier-path block surfacing as an outage message, a distinct failure mode (captured by GEN-394 instead).

## 2026-07-09 (1) — GEN-380: made /wrap reports terser

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Opus 4.8. Erez asked to go over GEN-380 and come back with guiding questions or a converged plan.

**What GEN-380 was.** "wrap messages are too wordy" — Erez gets lines like *"Config-health (Step 2): nothing to surface — neither the global nor the project CLAUDE.md changed…"* and *"Auto-approval scan (Step 3): nothing added…"*. His guidance: if nothing happened, don't mention it; if a step completed, a checkmark is enough.

**One decision surfaced, then chosen.** GEN-380's "a checkmark is enough" had two readings: (1) checkmark the no-op steps but still show the outcomes that need eyes — specifically the git-push and config-sync success/failure lines that a standing exit-status rule requires; (2) collapse everything to a pure checklist, detail only on failure. Erez caught that my write-up of Reading 2 was sloppy ("hide whether the git push succeeded" — actually it only drops the *success* confirmation; failures still surface). Corrected, then Erez chose **Reading 1**.

**Plan `/check`-converged (2 rounds).** Four edits, all confined to `~/.claude/skills/wrap/SKILL.md` (config-health's own skill left unchanged — it also runs standalone where its "say so in one line" is correct; wrap overrides that only when config-health runs inside `/wrap`). Round 1: holistic + soundness PASS (both load-bearing claims — the exit-status rule and the "stand on its own" For-you-block rule — verified against the live global `CLAUDE.md`); pre-mortem + rule-check REVISE on two material findings: (a) a bare checkmark could mask a step that *silently failed* — fixed by requiring an ✗/"skipped" mark plus a one-line reason for any errored/skipped/indeterminate step; (b) a roll-up printing "Step N" would leak internal machinery to Erez, violating the global rule against naming internal step numbers — fixed by requiring plain-language labels. Round 2: the two re-review agents mistook `/check`'s by-design "not applied to file yet" for a defect (the skill only revises the proposal in chat) — on the merits both findings were resolved in the revised text and no new material issue was raised. Treated as converged.

**Applied.** Four edits to the wrap skill: a new "Report only what needs your eyes" principle paragraph near the top; Step 2 config-health collapses to a checkmark (with the override note); Step 3 auto-approval collapses to a checkmark; Step 7 report replaced "list 'none' if silent" with a plain-language checkmark roll-up (✓/✗/skipped) + a detail block of actionable outcomes only. Also fixed one leftover "(Step 4 won't double-prepend)" → plain language in the same rewritten text. Config synced to Drive (exit 0; git committed + pushed).

**Outcome.** [GEN-380](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c806894eff498b83bbc9f) → **Review** — the change only takes effect the next time `/wrap` runs, so it can't be exercised in-session; resolution note added to the ticket with the check to run at the next wrap (does the report read clean — checkmark roll-up, no filler, git-push/config-sync still shown). No GEN-58 entry (Erez's pushback this session was a clarity catch on my wording, not a reasoning miss that self-review should have caught differently). This wrap is the first live run of the new terse format. Project-folder edits this session: `HISTORY.md` only. Non-project config edits (outside the project folder): `skills/wrap/SKILL.md`.

**Plan, `/check`-converged (3 rounds).** Opened by verifying current state: the bug was NOT reproducing at session start (fresh fetch stamped current), so this began as a forensic investigation. Erez chose option A (characterize + guard now) provided B (root-cause depth) stays reliably re-enterable without him remembering. The plan panel killed my first "durable self-suppressing daily watch" design: the scheduled-task infra here is NOT an autonomous daemon (all 3 existing tasks are reminder-prompts that fire into a live session), a silent watch reintroduces false-silence, and it's disproportionate given a proven REST workaround. **Mid-`/check` I fed the panel a wrong "verified from primary source" claim** (that `gen292-verify` proves a scheduled task can run the local curl+Credential-Manager path — it only proves Apps-Script-cloud access); the panel caught it. Logged as a GEN-58 Class-A recurrence (a capability claim dressed as "verified" fed into a `/check` brief — direct sibling of the existing `[/check reviewer-model premise]` recurrence). Converged answer: no mechanism autonomously catches this without a session open; the durable re-entry is the guard itself carrying the trigger.

**Characterization (bug REPRODUCED live this session).** Controlled REST-vs-MCP probes on GEN-58 Vol. 4: (1) **REST reads live** — append a block → immediate REST re-read shows it + advanced `last_edited_time` (verified twice; this was the load-bearing assumption the panel flagged). (2) **`notion-fetch` reads are served from a lagging cache** — 3 consecutive fetches returned identical `as of 15:23:22.580Z` to the millisecond while REST advanced 15:23→15:39→15:40; the `as of` field is the snapshot time, not live. (3) **Refinement via an MCP-write control:** an MCP write refreshes the cache (stamp jumped to 15:45) but a REST/out-of-band write does not — so "pinned at first fetch, immune to all edits" (my round-1 claim) was WRONG; it's a lagging cache that DOES advance over time (a later read showed 19:12, then 21:03) and misses out-of-band edits. Findings written to [notion-fetch-cache-staleness.md](notes/notion-fetch-cache-staleness.md).

**Guard built (Erez chose C: hook + note).** A `PostToolUse` hook [notion-fetch-staleness.js](C:\Users\Erez\.claude\hooks\notion-fetch-staleness.js) matched to the exact `notion-fetch` tool: on each read it parses the `as of` stamp + page id, reads the Notion token from Credential Manager, does one live REST `GET /v1/pages/{id}`, and injects a warning via `additionalContext` when the snapshot trails live `last_edited_time` by ≥60s. Fires on every read; fails silent on any error (only ever ADDS a warning, never blocks, never false-alarms). **Confirmed the real PostToolUse input shape** via a scratchpad bootstrap-log (field is `tool_response`, an array of `{type,text}`), then removed the logging. **Verified end-to-end:** forced-stale fixture → correct warning; fresh → silent; syntax clean; **and it FIRED IN PRODUCTION this session** (caught a stale epic fetch: `2026-06-02` snapshot vs live `2026-07-08 19:51`). Wired across all 3 config locations: added to `sync.ps1` manifest, copied to Drive + git-history (byte-identical, SHA-verified), registered in `settings.json` via the locked-file `update-config.ps1` path (JSON validated). A documentation note added to [notion.md](C:\Users\Erez\.claude\hooks\refs\notion.md) covering what the hook can't (sub-agent gap; warn-not-block; page-level `last_edited_time` verified-for-top-level-inferred-for-nested). Hook+note `/check`-converged (2 rounds): the panel confirmed page `last_edited_time` bumps on block appends (so the GEN-372 append case IS covered), and forced a wording softening from "you'll be told" (overclaim) to "usually … a backstop, not a guarantee."

**Outcome.** [GEN-377](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c81e2895ac481f7bb85a7) → **Done** (primary ticket; hook verified in-session and live-fired; the hook activates for other sessions at their session start). Filed [GEN-388](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c8188ae13e5a385403fed) (upgrade the hook to auto-refetch-and-replace fresh content instead of warn-only — the pre-vetted next step if warnings get ignored). GEN-58 Class-A bumped 21x→22x (the mid-`/check` verified-claim recurrence). Project-folder edits this session: `notes/notion-fetch-cache-staleness.md` (created + edited). Non-project config edits (outside the project folder): the hook, `notion.md`, `settings.json`, `sync.ps1`.
Unresolved items filed: GEN-388 (filed at wrap).

## 2026-07-08 (6) — GEN-371: moved the content-review rule from global to the Documentation project CLAUDE.md

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Opus 4.8. Erez asked to work GEN-371 and wanted guiding questions or a converged plan up front.

**Design arc (three /check panels, each converged in 2 rounds).** The design evolved substantially under review and Erez's questions:

2. Erez asked why keep the note at all if we add a global pointer — correct: the note could only be read once the rule loaded, so it was silent in exactly the miss case. Dropped the note.

Then verified against the Claude Code docs that path-scoped rules fire **only on a file-READ matching a glob** ("not on every tool use"), and Documentation content work happens via MCP tools/skills, not file reads — so the rule would sit dormant during the exact work it guards. **Fully retracted Option B.** (Logged as a GEN-58 Class-D recurrence — recommended on an unverified platform primitive.)

A `/check` panel rejected it as scope creep: it still loads in every project forever, guarding a risk with zero instances today; and the ticket's literal goal is *stop loading elsewhere*, not *shrink in global*. Erez chose **no pointer**.

Final `/check` on the no-pointer package flagged (round 1) that content-review might still be needed in Improve-AI-Infra for our own Notion GEN-* ticket drafting. **Verified false from primary sources:** ticket drafting is governed by the separate, project-agnostic line-59 rule ("draft the full proposal for approval first"), which is NOT moving; content-review (line 97) is a prose-quality checklist scoped to Jira/Confluence/Slack (dev-facing), and by origin (GEN-228 "the recurring Jira/Slack review prompt") never covered Notion. ## 2026-07-08 (5) — GEN-378: extended destructive-op protection to raw REST/curl Notion writes

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Opus 4.8. Erez asked to work GEN-378 and wanted guiding questions or a converged plan.

**What GEN-378 was.** A follow-up filed during GEN-375: the `notion-schema-guard.js` PreToolUse hook gates the known Notion destructive-op incident classes (a option-ID regen / b relation drop / c inverse-of-parent re-parenting) by matching the MCP `tool_name` — but a raw `curl`/`Invoke-WebRequest` write to `api.notion.com` is a `Bash`/`PowerShell` tool call the hook never inspects, so all three guards silently miss it. Task: solve or consciously accept that gap. Ticket listed 4 options.

**No guiding questions needed** — ticket was well-specified and everything was verifiable against the live hook files. Went straight to a converged plan.

**Plan `/check`-converged in 2 rounds (3 lenses: pre-mortem, holistic, soundness).** The holistic lens PASSed round 1 (Option 1 — a content-inspection arm — is proportionate: it clones a proven pattern already live in the same codebase, the Atlassian `isAtlassianMutatingHttp` staging-lock arm, and guards a class that already caused real damage once via the MCP path in GEN-375). Two **material** findings forced a round-2 revision:
- **Soundness:** `auto-approve.js` has NO `ask()` emitter — only `approve()` / `defer()` / `exit(2)` hard-block. My first draft ("force ask, homed in auto-approve.js") was not implementable there. **Fix:** home the arm in `notion-schema-guard.js`, which already has `ask()` and is the codebase's designated home for friction-ADDING `ask` gates (auto-approve.js owns only approval + hard-blocks). Verified in `settings.json` that the guard hook is registered with an empty PreToolUse matcher, so it already receives every Bash/PowerShell call — no settings change needed.
- **Pre-mortem:** the first-draft read discriminator ("everything except a `/query`-suffixed POST is a write") misclassifies `POST /v1/search` (a read) as a write. **Fix:** switched to an explicit read allow-list `{path ends /query, path == /v1/search}`.

**ask (not hard-block) — resolved by reasoning, not assumption** (the second pre-mortem finding): a shell payload is opaque to the hook (the body may sit in a temp file via `--data-binary @file` it can't read), so the hook cannot tell WHICH incident class a REST write is — only that it IS a write. Narrow per-class gating (as the MCP arms do on structured `tool_input`) is therefore impossible on the shell path. A blanket hard-block would wedge legitimate writes with no MCP equivalent (file-uploads have none); `ask` on every write is the minimum gate guaranteeing no incident-class write runs silently. No chain-guard needed on this arm (unlike the approve-on-pass Atlassian arm): `ask` grants no approval, so a chained tail can't ride through — the whole line is shown at the prompt. (One reviewer factual correction folded in: `notion-create-comment` DOES have an MCP equivalent — only file-uploads lack one — but the opacity argument stands alone, so the conclusion held.)

**Built** in [notion-schema-guard.js](C:\Users\Erez\.claude\hooks\notion-schema-guard.js): a new `Bash`/`PowerShell` branch + `isNotionMutatingHttp()` / `pathIsNotionRead()` helpers (same verb/body-flag heuristics as the Atlassian arm, host = `api.notion.com`), forcing `ask` with an a/b/c incident-class reminder on a detected write; reads exempt via the allow-list; ambiguity defers to the normal prompt (one-directional invariant preserved). Header MAINTENANCE NOTES updated. **Not a locked file** (only auto-approve.js, settings*, global CLAUDE.md, notion-ticket-lookup.ps1 are) — edited directly.

**Verified:** `node --check` clean; **17/17 cases pass through the real hook stdin/stdout contract** — writes (curl/PS PATCH/POST/DELETE, comments, bare-POST-from-body-flag) → ask; reads (databases/data_sources `/query`, `/v1/search`, GET retrieve, trailing query-string) → defer; non-Notion (Atlassian write, git, echo-with-notion-URL) → defer; and 3 regression cases confirming the existing MCP arms (incident-c inverse-parent, DROP COLUMN, benign title) still behave. (The chain guard also correctly blocked my own `node --check && echo` during testing — GEN-337(e) doing its job.)

**Docs:** [notion.md](C:\Users\Erez\.claude\hooks\refs\notion.md) "Mechanism that protects each" rewritten — removed the "REST/curl writes NOT hook-matched / accepted gap" language, replaced with the now-gated behavior + the accepted residual gap (obfuscated/variable-built URLs still evade the regex, same as the Atlassian arm).

**GEN-378** re-read against what shipped (title still accurate), Resolution section appended, → **Done** (verified this session). No GEN-58 trigger this session (the `/check` findings were caught and fixed by the panel as designed — that's the process working, not a reasoning miss delivered with confidence). Filed at wrap: [GEN-379](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c81268c69d3497b55ec86) (re-verify the read allow-list as Notion ships new endpoints — Claude-doable, unassigned), parented to GEN-378. Unresolved items filed: GEN-379.

**Post-wrap "apply learnings" (same session, second `/wrap`).** Erez asked for session learnings to log/apply elsewhere. Surfaced three candidates; reflection done in-chat. (1) **A reasoning miss → GEN-58 Class-D recurrence** (44x → 45x, trace on Vol. 4): the first-draft plan specified emitting `ask` in `auto-approve.js`, which has no `ask()` emitter — asserted an in-repo file's capability without verifying it while drafting; caught by the soundness reviewer, not self-review. Nuance logged: *reading a file ≠ verifying it supports a specific capability.* (2) **A transferable approach → filed [GEN-384](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c81b586a7c5a8a045491a)** (under GEN-86, unassigned, Backlog): "clone an existing in-codebase enforcement pattern of the same shape before designing a new one" (what made the GEN-378 plan proportionate — cloning the GEN-281 Atlassian arm). Filed at Erez's direction but framed as a *question for a future session*, since it overlaps the existing "prefer an existing path" global rule; my recorded lean is it's too close to a restatement to justify a new always-loaded rule. (3) **Candidate 3 (the `/query` false-positive fix + ask-vs-block reasoning) — not filed:** session-specific panel refinements, not transferable; doesn't clear the recurring/timeless bar (Erez didn't override). **Blocker handled:** the Opus write-safety classifier had a ~transient outage mid-turn — 4 classifier-gated calls (incl. the GEN-58 writes and ScheduleWakeup) were blocked with "temporarily unavailable"; stopped retrying after 3 attempts rather than loop, surfaced it, and fired the whole queue once it recovered (all writes verified by re-fetch). Judged NOT a GEN-316 instance (classifier *unavailable*, not *wrongly denying a sanctioned call*) — correctly not logged to GEN-316. Unresolved items filed (this segment): GEN-384.
