No task in the opening message — Erez explained he'd used the desktop folder picker to start a session and, instead of his three projects, saw a home directory full of folders (some Claude-related), and asked why Claude files/folders exist outside the `.claude` folder.

**Investigation.** Read the actual filesystem. Established two categories: (1) real config at fixed, app-owned paths that cannot move — `.claude\` (main config), `.claude.json` + `.claude.json.backup` (main app config file, rewritten frequently), `.cache\claude\`; (2) custom siblings from this project with hardcoded references — `.claude-git-history\` (a live git repo backing up config; referenced across scripts/skills) and `.claude-staging\` (the `/staging` store; path hardcoded in `auto-approve.js` and named in the `/staging` skill). A single parent folder for everything is impossible because the app reads (1) from fixed paths.

**Reasoning miss #1 (corrected after pushback).** The first answer confidently asserted it was all one `.claude` folder and the picker was merely browsing the filesystem. Erez pushed back with a screenshot showing multiple distinct Claude-related folders AND loose files. Only then did on-disk verification happen. The loose files turned out to be junk: `fpcreate*.txt`, `spaces*.txt`, `forgehelp.txt` (all captured `node.exe` NODE_TLS_REJECT_UNAUTHORIZED stderr dumps) and `readable_gen58.txt` (a dumped Notion page). Root cause: past sessions redirected command output into the home directory instead of the scratchpad. Erez deleted them himself.

**Clarified the real goal.** Not "everything under `.claude`" but a tidy home directory. Presented options: (A) fold the custom siblings into `.claude` + fix refs; (B) hide the dotfolders; (C) the picker was showing hidden items. When Erez confirmed it was about clutter and asked for "all Claude's stuff in one folder," laid out symlink/junction consolidation vs a shortcut folder vs hide-and-partial-consolidate.

**Reasoning miss #2 (corrected after pushback).** Recommended Windows directory junctions (Option 1) as "the answer" while admitting in the same breath that the core assumption — the desktop app tolerating its config path being a junction — was unverified. Erez: "Why didn't you check your suggestion?" Fair hit. **Then actually tested it, non-destructively, in the session scratchpad:** created a junction, wrote a file through it, confirmed the write physically landed in the real target, and confirmed a target file was readable through the junction — the transparent pass-through consolidation relies on. Cleaned up (removed the junction with `rmdir` first so removal didn't follow into the target, then deleted the scratch dir). **Verified:** directory junctions work on this machine (NTFS, no admin needed). **Still unverified (needs a live test, can't be settled from a scratch folder):** whether the app reads its own config cleanly through a junction, and whether git behaves across a junction for `.claude-git-history`. **Hard limitation found:** `.claude.json` is a loose *file*, not a folder — junctions only work on directories, so consolidating it would need an admin/Developer-Mode symlink; realistic outcome is "every Claude *folder* in one place, minus that one config file."

**Outcome — nothing shipped, all filed for later.** Per Erez's instruction ("file this into notion with all the needed context for a later session, don't show it to me, then run wrap"): appended the full consolidation approach (constraint, tested junction findings, what's unverified, the `.claude.json` limitation, an incremental safest-first execution plan, and the rejected alternatives) to [GEN-373](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c81c18193e0b5532ef888), which already held the auto-delete-junk context filed earlier in the session. No consolidation was executed and Erez has not approved executing it. The two reasoning misses are the same underlying failure mode — presenting an unverified belief as a settled conclusion, caught by the user rather than self-review — filed as [GEN-374](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c81b5a1b6df53c264702f) for a future session to classify and log on GEN-58 (the wrap deliberately doesn't do the interactive GEN-58 classification).

**Post-wrap — a real error the wrap itself introduced, caught by Erez.** Erez checked Notion and found GEN-374 had no parent and GEN-86 (the epic) had been made a *sub-item* of GEN-374. Cause: when filing GEN-373 and GEN-374 during Step 1, the parent was set using the wrong property — `Related to Team-Tasks (Parent item)` (the inverse-of-parent relation) with GEN-86 as the value — which re-parents the *referenced* page rather than childing the new ticket. This is exactly the injected Notion incident-class (c); the safeguard's snapshot-then-verify was applied at edit time in other contexts but NOT at ticket-*creation*, and the post-create verify only confirmed the value landed on the new ticket, never re-fetching GEN-86 to confirm the epic's own parent was untouched — so it went silent until Erez saw it. GEN-373 was actually fully orphaned (no parent at all). **Repair:** cleared GEN-86's erroneous `Parent item`; set GEN-373 and GEN-374 each to `Parent item = GEN-86` (correct field/direction; cleared GEN-374's bad inverse value); re-fetched all three and confirmed GEN-86 is top-level again with both tickets in its children list. Correct hierarchy restored: GEN-86 → GEN-373, GEN-374. **Filed [GEN-375](https://app.notion.com/p/3976e495d07c81e9a056ccec81fe62f8)** (under GEN-86, using `Parent item` directly, and verified GEN-86 was NOT re-parented this time): investigate the exact create-time re-parenting mechanism, codify always-use-`Parent item`-never-the-inverse (at creation too), extend post-create verification to re-fetch the parent, and consider whether `notion-schema-guard.js` should also match `notion-create-pages` writes to a `(Parent item)`-suffixed relation, not just `update_properties`. Same underlying failure mode as GEN-374 (had the warning in context, still misapplied it at creation), now with a concrete Notion-mechanism instance.

Round 2/3 also fixed two wording defects in the resident replacement text (a stale "all Notion-specific so far" census claim; an "or any other destructive op" clause that read as demanding the Notion refs file before non-Notion ops like `git push --force`) and confirmed the four less-common safe-list tool names (`notion-download-attachment`, `notion-get-async-task`, `notion-query-meeting-notes`, `notion-query-database-view`) against this session's own live MCP tool inventory, backstopped by a deny-by-default failure direction regardless.

## 2026-07-07 (1) — Sync-safety cluster: GEN-355 (primary) → Done; GEN-353/GEN-219/GEN-248 → Done (proposed)

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra). Opened on the ambient "ai infra" prompt with no task → listed live open state pulled fresh from Notion (SQL query mode is plan-gated here, so this went through the Credential-Manager REST lookup script instead). Erez asked what to work on; recommended the sync-safety cluster (GEN-355 + GEN-353, folding in the older overlapping GEN-248 and GEN-219) over finishing [GEN-337](https://app.notion.com/p/3916e495d07c8140bf3ee4e493ceb760)'s remaining candidate or the [GEN-331](https://app.notion.com/p/3906e495d07c814eb5e6dcb9ce06096f) decision — both headline tickets came from real incidents that week (a silent edit-loss and a near-miss sync race), nothing protects against either today, and one verification run could cover all four tickets since they live in the same two scripts.

**Plan — `/check` (pre-mortem+premise-challenge, holistic, soundness), converged in 3 rounds.** Round 1: holistic PASS (advisory only); pre-mortem and soundness both found material issues in the first draft, which trusted Google Drive File Stream's `LastWriteTime` to detect "which side changed" (unverified, and independently documented to be unreliable — Drive's mount can rewrite mtimes independent of real edits) and gave the new `sync.ps1` concurrency lock unbounded hold time (a stalled `/wrap` push, a bare caller found via live grep, could starve every other session's config edits indefinitely) plus an unspecified give-up signal (an exit-0 give-up would let `update-config.ps1` silently proceed on stale state, reopening the exact bug being fixed). Round 2: redesigned Fix A around a local SHA-256 baseline manifest (no Drive timestamps consulted at all) and wrapped the git network calls in a background job with a ~90s timeout plus a distinct exit code (6) on lock-wait give-up; soundness PASS (verified the exit-6 composition against the live `update-config.ps1` code and the baseline state-machine's "don't update on KEEP" correctness); pre-mortem narrowed to one finding: `Stop-Job` doesn't reliably kill the underlying `git.exe`, risking an orphaned process or a leftover `.git\index.lock` that would break the design's own "self-heals next run" safety net. Round 3: added an explicit kill-orphan-then-remove-lock sequence plus a defensive start-of-run stale-lock sweep — converged, PASS.

**Build (Sonnet 5, per Erez's model choice after the design converged — the remaining work was implementing a fully-specified plan, not open-ended judgment).** Rewrote [sync.ps1](G:\My Drive\AI Projects\_Tooling\Claude\sync.ps1): the `To-Claude` pull now compares each file's content hash against `sync-baseline.json` (a new per-machine manifest) instead of blindly overwriting — local-edited-not-yet-pushed is kept with a warning, both-sides-changed is kept local with a loud CONFLICT warning (Drive's copy isn't lost, just not applied), a `-DiscardLocal` switch restores the old blind-overwrite for the one legitimate discard flow. Added a `sync.lock` (separate from the config lock; ordering strictly config→sync, verified no reverse path exists) with heartbeat-logged wait and exit code 6 on give-up; git pull/push run in a timed background job with orphan-process cleanup and stale-`index.lock` repair. Updated [update-config.ps1](G:\My Drive\AI Projects\_Tooling\Claude\update-config.ps1): config-lock retry widened from ~1.5s to ~3 minutes with heartbeats, and the pending-sync recovery message now names `-DiscardLocal` (keeping the marker-deletion step, which still gates entry).

**Testing — sandboxed, before touching production.** Built a parameterized copy of both scripts pointed at a throwaway sandbox tree (fake local/Drive/git-history folders) and ran 11+ scenarios through the real mechanics: first-run baseline seeding; the original incident reproduced and fixed (a local edit survives a pull); Drive-newer freshness pull; the both-changed conflict case; `-DiscardLocal` override; two overlapping syncs (second waits, then succeeds); the give-up path; a simulated hung git push against an unreachable remote (times out cleanly, no leftover lock, commit lands locally); the stale-`index.lock` defensive cleanup; a local-only file surviving a pull; and a full `update-config.ps1` round trip (including waiting through a competing `sync.lock`). **Caught and fixed one real bug the review panel could not have found:** a `Write-Host`-vs-`Write-Output` mistake in the lock-wait heartbeat meant that once a heartbeat line fired, PowerShell's implicit pipeline capture corrupted the function's returned lock handle into something without `.Close()`/`.Dispose()` methods — found via the give-up-path test, fixed, and re-verified clean across the full suite.

**Deploy.** Applied both files via `update-config.ps1 -Op write-file` (exit 0 each; pulled → wrote → pushed to Drive + git history). Hash-verified both deployed files byte-identical to the sandbox-tested copies. Live-fired a real `To-Claude` pull against production immediately after: 42 files tracked, clean no-op, exit 0 — confirming the new baseline-manifest logic works correctly against the real `.claude` folder, not just the sandbox.

**Tickets.**
- [GEN-355](https://app.notion.com/p/3956e495d07c8131ae05ccb5d1be7b10) → **Done** (primary ticket; resolution written, fully verified in-session).
- [GEN-353](https://app.notion.com/p/3956e495d07c8150a8d6ff0354e74627) → **Done** (proposed; resolution written).
- [GEN-219](https://app.notion.com/p/37b6e495d07c81ceaa26f097583450d3) → **Done** (proposed; resolution written).

## 2026-07-05 (3) — GEN-337 candidate (e) BUILT + live-verified: hard-block mixed-risk shell chains, plus cross-session prevention via the existing shell-refs injector

Session on project 3 (Improve AI Infra). Opened with the ambient prompt "ai infra" (no task) → listed the project's open state; Erez picked candidate (e) (command-chaining enforcement hook) and asked "is e fully vetted, or should we run check on it?" I confirmed it was only *filed*, never designed — needs `/check`. Model progression this session: Fable 5 → (mid-design) Fable 5/high → Opus 4.8 (Erez's `/model` before `/wrap`).

**The reasoning correction that shaped the design.** Before building, Erez pushed on the *prevention* half of his goal across three turns ("hard block + reduce the chance a mixed chain even happens" → "make the learning carry over session to session" → "both don't get us where we want; suggest what I should do"). My first framing offered a false two-option fork: a **net-new** `UserPromptSubmit` injection hook (which I dismissed as the pattern the candidate-(g) panel had just rejected) vs. "the resident `CLAUDE.md` rule already carries it." His pushback + the model switch triggered a from-scratch re-examination that surfaced the miss: **`inject-shell-refs.js` is a live, in-production injection channel** firing at every session's first shell command — so cross-session prevention costs *one line* in `hooks/refs/shell.md` with zero new machinery, and the (g) rejection (grounds: net-new standing machinery for a footprint-shrink task) never applied to an existing channel. Logged as a **GEN-58 Class-C recurrence** ([mechanism-family single-instance survey]: ruling out a design direction without enumerating live sibling mechanisms) — index count 11x, Vol. 3 trace, both writes verified.

**Design — `/check` (3 lenses: pre-mortem, holistic, soundness), converged round 2.** Round 1 raised 5 material findings, all fixed: (1) quote/escape-stripping was under-specified → wrote a concrete per-shell scanner (bash backslash escapes, POSIX single-quote no-escape, PS backtick escapes, PS doubled `''`); (2) guard-error behavior unspecified → explicit try/catch, fail-open-to-a-PROMPT (never a bare throw = exit 1 = non-blocking); (3) ordering omitted `enforceStaging` as the real first gate — and that surfaced a genuine **pre-existing bypass**: a chained Atlassian write with a valid staging pass would `approve()` the whole line (smuggled tail included) before the shell branch ran; (4)/(5) the "just split it" refusal advice is impossible for atomic control-flow one-liners → carve-out to a prompt. Both round-2 lenses PASS.

**Build (all in [auto-approve.js](C:\Users\Erez\.claude\hooks\auto-approve.js)).** `scanChain(command, tool)` — single-line, quote-aware, per-shell scanner returning NO-CHAIN / CHAINED / AMBIGUOUS (quoted spans blanked so a `;` in a JSON body or commit message never counts; `$(`/`${`/bash-backtick/heredoc/unbalanced-quote → AMBIGUOUS). `blockMixedChain()` — CHAINED + not-every-segment-safe + not a control-flow construct → `exit(2)` with an educational refusal; AMBIGUOUS/multi-line/error/all-safe → fall through to the normal prompt. `enforceStaging`'s shell branch now runs the scanner *before* pass matching (CHAINED → block, pass NOT consumed). Blocks logged to a new `chain-blocks.jsonl` (redacted; NOT the deferred-calls log). Companion: a one-line reminder in `hooks/refs/shell.md` (injected each session by `inject-shell-refs.js`), and a header note in that hook flagging the one deliberate safety-rule exception.

**Verification.** 23/23 fixture cases through the *real hook integration* (mixed chains block in both shells; quoted/escaped separators ignored; `$()`/unbalanced → prompt; control-flow one-liners → prompt; all-safe chains still auto-approve; staged-Atlassian clean write still consumes a pass; chained staged write blocked with pass unconsumed; sync exact-match still approves; protected-file block + self-check intact). Applied to the locked file via `update-config.ps1 -Op write-file` (exit 0; pulled → overwrote → pushed to Drive + git → lock released); installed file hash-verified byte-identical to the tested copy. **Live-proven:** the guard fired twice on my *own* subsequent commands (a chained hash check; my `; Write-Output "…EXIT: $LASTEXITCODE"` idiom on mutating scripts), each correctly refused.

**Backup gap closed in passing.** `inject-notion-refs.js`, `inject-shell-refs.js`, `deny-askuserquestion.js` were registered in `settings.json` but in no backup — added all three to `sync.ps1`'s `$FileMap` (+ header doc); sync ran clean, committed + pushed; confirmed present at Drive. Systemic backup-gap tracker [GEN-140](https://app.notion.com/p/3746e495d07c81bab600ce35574d8498) still open.

**Tickets.**
- [GEN-337](https://app.notion.com/p/3916e495d07c8140bf3ee4e493ceb760) — candidate (e) BUILT + verified; progress note appended + re-fetched. **Kept In Progress:** (f) destructive-ops snapshot hook still open; (a) deferred. Part 3 of (e) (shrink the resident rule to a pointer) deliberately deferred to its own step/review.

## 2026-07-05 (1) — Break-glass override info made durable in the lock hook (two-layer guard, `/check`-converged over 4 Opus rounds, built + applied + live-tested)

Session opened on project 3 (Improve AI Infra), Opus 4.8. Erez pointed at an open loose end from the prior session: *"the lock hook's refusal message is now load-bearing for the break-glass override info, so a future edit mustn't strip it — suggest how to tackle this deterministically."*

**The problem.** Prior session's GEN-337 candidate (d) shrank the resident global `CLAUDE.md` locked-config rule so the emergency-override string (`CLAUDE_CONFIG_UNLOCK=1` env var / `.config-unlock` sentinel) no longer lives there — the resident rule now only *points* at `hooks/auto-approve.js`'s refusal message. That made the message the SOLE durable home for the override info, with nothing catching a future edit that strips it.

**Design — `/check` panel, 4 rounds on Opus, materially reshaped my answer twice:**
- *Round 1:* my first proposal (Option A: a file-wide grep at `/wrap` for the override tokens) drew two REVISE verdicts — the grep matches the token in BOTH the runtime logic and the message, so it'd pass even if the message were gutted (false negative). It also rested on a **non-existent "refs-health /wrap check"** I'd assumed from a HISTORY phrasing (self-caught by reading the live `/wrap` skill + hooks before briefing the panel). Holistic proposed a structurally stronger fix: shared named constants so the message can't drift from the logic.
- *Round 2:* revised to Layer 1 (shared `UNLOCK_ENV_VAR` constant) + Layer 2 (in-hook self-check). Holistic flagged that Layer 2 should be an in-hook self-test, not a `/wrap` sub-step (fires every session, less code, refactor-proof).
- *Round 3:* both lenses caught a NEW defect in my in-hook check — a bare `throw` at module load exits 1, which for a PreToolUse hook is NON-blocking → fails OPEN (verified against Claude Code hook exit-code semantics: only `exit 2` blocks). A tripped self-check would then let the bad edit through — worse than the bug it guards.
- *Round 4 (Opus):* self-check switched to `process.stderr.write(...); process.exit(2)` (fail-closed, same as `blockIfProtected`), wrapped so any internal error also routes to `exit(2)`. Both lenses PASS → converged. Confirmed edges: the self-check is over static constants (deterministically all-pass or all-fail per version, never intermittent); "wedge until fixed" is the desired behavior for a last-resort guard; out-of-band `CLAUDE_CONFIG_UNLOCK=1` recovers a wedged session without the blocked tool-call channel. Accepted residual: a semantic gutting that keeps the raw tokens but strips the human-readable prose is outside deterministic tooling's reach (stated, not closed).

**Build.** Added `const UNLOCK_ENV_VAR = 'CLAUDE_CONFIG_UNLOCK'` (used in `configUnlocked()` and the message); factored the message into `buildRefusalMessage(p)` called unmodified by `blockIfProtected`; added the on-load self-check IIFE. Verified on a working copy before touching the locked file: `node --check` OK; `diff` = only the three intended changes; behavioral tests — benign call approved (self-check silent), protected-file edit blocks with both tokens present, and a **simulated gutted message → self-check fails closed (exit 2) with a clear diagnostic**. Applied to the live locked file via `update-config.ps1 -Op write-file` (exit 0; pulled, overwrote, pushed to Drive + git, lock released); re-verified the installed file byte-identical + live-retested.

**GEN-58.** Logged a Class-D recurrence (designed on an unverified primitive: the assumed "refs-health /wrap check") — dated trace on Vol. 3, index count 40→41. Self-caught before it reached the final answer.

**Tickets.**
- [GEN-337](https://app.notion.com/p/3916e495d07c8140bf3ee4e493ceb760) — progress note appended; **kept In Progress**. Corrected my own too-hasty prior-turn "it'd go to Done": the ticket is a multi-candidate tracker (shrink candidates a–g) with four still open — this work was follow-on hardening of an already-done candidate, not ticket completion. Remaining: (a) ticket-workflow rules (deferred), (e) command-chaining hook, (f) destructive-ops snapshot hook, (g) GEN-58-protocol→skill.
- [GEN-345](https://app.notion.com/p/3946e495d07c812eb20cd08fdc5f2f63) (filed, Backlog, unassigned, under GEN-86) — guard against declaring a multi-candidate/epic-like ticket Done when only one part is complete; the existing title/description re-read rule doesn't cover the multi-open-candidate case.

Revised to a **one-line pointer** (matching the file's existing Notion/shell-notes extraction pattern) instead of full deletion, with the deny message quoted verbatim and diffed element-by-element against the rule. Round 2: all three findings confirmed RESOLVED.

Sub-agent coverage checked via a dedicated sub-agent test: the tool doesn't exist for sub-agents at the platform level ("No such tool available"), so there's no gap to cover. Resident rule at global `CLAUDE.md` line 131 then replaced with the panel-approved one-line pointer via `update-global-rule.ps1` (exit 0, verified) — net saving ≈190 chars, corrected down from the ~340 first estimated in (9)'s survey.

## 2026-07-02 (7) — GEN-336 designed and built: reference-injection hooks now warn on failure instead of failing silently, plus an every-`/wrap` backstop check

Picked up [GEN-336](https://app.notion.com/p/3916e495d07c81f4914ee71bb18750e3) (filed in (6)): both `inject-notion-refs.js` and `inject-shell-refs.js` fail open by design, so a moved/renamed/emptied `hooks/refs/*.md` file would silently and permanently drop content that used to be always-loaded — nothing else would catch the gap.

**Design, reviewed to convergence before writing code (`/check`, 3 lenses — pre-mortem+premise, holistic, soundness+grounding — Sonnet reviewers, 2 rounds).** Two-layer plan: Layer A edits both hooks to warn (via `additionalContext`) instead of staying silent when their trigger fires and the notes file is missing/empty, never blocking the call; Layer B adds a new deterministic check to `config-health-scan.ps1`, run unconditionally at every `/wrap` (not gated behind the weekly settings-nudge or the CLAUDE.md-changed condition), that verifies every registered hook exists, every notes file a hook's code references exists and is non-empty, and — an idiom-drift guard — every notes file under `hooks/refs/` is claimed by some hook's extracted reference.

**Round 1 caught two real, material problems.** Pre-mortem: the plan asserted Layer B fit "existing practice" by running at every `/wrap`, but the closest precedent (`deadHookPaths`) runs only in the on-demand settings scan, not the automatic weekly nudge — the plan hadn't explicitly chosen between every-wrap cost and the weekly-cadence detection gap. Soundness: the proposed extraction regex (`refs[\/][\w.-]+\.md` over raw hook source) would have matched only the hooks' header **comments**, not their actual `path.join(__dirname, 'refs', 'x.md')` code — a check that "passes" by coincidence of comment wording, exactly the kind of silent, undetected failure the ticket exists to close. Holistic lens passed with an advisory (a simpler flat convention-check would work as well as the regex-based one for today's two hooks; noted, not required to fix, since the corrected regex plus the idiom-drift guard already closes the false-negative hole).

**Fixes:** explicitly chose every-`/wrap` cadence for Layer B and stated why (pure file-stat + regex over ~9 short files, no agent spawn — unlike the settings scan's agent-spawning judgment pass the weekly gate protects); corrected the regex to parse the actual `path.join` call shape, resolved per-hook-directory; specified Layer A's missing/empty check as an explicit branch inside the try block (not error-type inference in the catch), so the outer catch-all stays silent for genuinely unexpected errors. **Round 2: both re-reviewers confirmed RESOLVED** — the soundness reviewer verified by direct inspection that the corrected regex matches the real code lines (not comments) in both hooks.

**Built + tested.** Layer A: both hooks now `fs.existsSync` the notes file before reading, and check for empty/whitespace-only content after; either condition emits a one-line warning ("...refs/shell.md missing or empty — shell reference notes were NOT loaded; flag this to Erez") instead of silence. Shell hook still writes its once-per-session marker on the warn branch (warns once per session); Notion hook has no marker (warns on every matching prompt while broken — a deliberate, bounded trade-off, self-clearing once fixed). Layer B: new `-Action refs-health` in `config-health-scan.ps1`, wired into `config-health`'s auto mode unconditionally; testable via new `-RefsHealthSettingsJson`/`-RefsHealthHooksDir` params pointing at a fixture instead of the real config.

**Fixture testing caught a real bug.** Built a fabricated broken fixture (missing refs file, empty refs file, an orphaned refs file, a dead hook path) — the first run threw "Index operation failed; the array index evaluated to null." Root cause: `(Resolve-Path -LiteralPath $missingFile).Path` returns `$null` in PS 5.1 when the path doesn't exist, and `$null.Path` throws. Fixed by falling back to the raw joined path as the dedup key when `Resolve-Path` returns nothing. Re-ran: all four fixture scenarios correctly flagged, and the healthy file (`present.md`) correctly excluded from the orphan list. Ran against the real config: `healthy: true`, `referencedCount: 2` — confirms the regex matches the real code in both real hooks, not their comments.

**Layer A verified via scratchpad copies** (missing-refs and empty-refs directories, distinct fake session IDs per test) — both hooks warn correctly; the shell hook's once-per-session marker correctly suppresses a second call in the same fake session. Healthy-path re-test against the real hook location (fresh fake session ID) confirmed real content still injects unchanged. All fake session markers cleaned up afterward (verified via the actual `os.tmpdir()`-resolved path, `/tmp`, after an initial cleanup attempt targeted the wrong Windows path and missed them).

**Applied + verified:** hooks and `config-health-scan.ps1`/`SKILL.md` are not locked files — edited directly. GEN-336 updated with a build summary and moved to **Done** (primary ticket of this session's work; no verification deferred, since everything was tested end-to-end this session).

**Four-state status:**
- *Done + verified:* both hooks patched and warn-tested; `refs-health` check built, bug-fixed, and verified against both a broken fixture and the real live config; wired into `config-health`'s auto-mode documentation (`SKILL.md`).
- *Verified-how:* `/check` panel to convergence (2 rounds, 2 material findings fixed); fixture-driven tests for both layers; direct re-fetch of GEN-336 confirming body + Status landed.
- *Still open:* everything carried from (6)'s still-open list (the GEN-334 timing-claim correction itself — still just a note, not applied; whether to survey for further CLAUDE.md compression clusters or call that workstream done; size-gate, GEN-333 rescope, Erez's Notion-coverage check, GEN-335's `/check` skill edit).
- *Deliberately not done:* editing GEN-334's body (still deferred, per Erez's standing instruction from (6)); the holistic lens's simpler flat-convention alternative for Layer B (accepted as a valid non-blocking alternative, not switched to, since the corrected regex + idiom-drift guard already closes its stated gap).

## 2026-07-02 (6) — Shell reference block extracted via a `PreToolUse` hook: a documented limit turned out to be wrong, unblocking a deferred cluster; skill cleanup caught by round-1 review

Picked up the item (5) left open ("survey for more, or call the shrink-half done") by tackling the one deferred, larger-than-any-single-cluster item: the shell/PowerShell gotchas block, held back since 2026-07-02 (1)/(2) because a `UserPromptSubmit` hook (the Notion pattern) is the wrong trigger for shell work, which rarely announces itself in the prompt. The right trigger is the tool call itself — but 2026-07-01 (4) and 2026-07-02 (1) both recorded (and GEN-334 still states) that `PreToolUse` `additionalContext` only lands *after* a tool call, too late to help the first shell command of a session.

**That premise was wrong.** Sent a docs-verification agent to re-check `code.claude.com/docs/en/hooks` directly rather than trust the carried-forward claim (per the "no capability claims from memory" rule). The docs say the opposite: `PreToolUse` `additionalContext` is described as "context next to the tool call" (delivered before/alongside execution) — it's `PostToolUse` that lands "next to the tool result." This **reverses** a stated limit from two prior entries and from GEN-334's own body. Filed the correction as a note-for-later on the new ticket below rather than silently editing GEN-334.

**Built + live-fire verified:** `hooks/inject-shell-refs.js` (`PreToolUse`, matcher `Bash|PowerShell` in `settings.json`) + `hooks/refs/shell.md` (11 non-dangerous bullets). Once-per-session via a session-ID-keyed marker file; fails open. Confirmed working *in this actual session* — the hook fired on this session's own first shell command and its injected content appeared in context, not just a synthetic test.

**Classification:** kept 3 bullets resident (literal-`$` interpolation, `String.Replace(x, $null)` mass-deletion, empty-string-arg-drop) — silent/destructive failure modes where the hook's timing gap (notes arrive *alongside*, not *before*, the very first shell command) is unacceptable. Moved 11 bullets whose failure modes are visible and recoverable (encoding, exit-code handling, quoting, curl/wget alias, and — after explicit user approval to include it — the Google Drive script-signing note, since hitting that error is itself a shell call, so the hook has already fired by the time the note would matter).

**4-lens `/check` panel (pre-mortem+premise, holistic, soundness+cross-ref, rule-check; Sonnet): 3 PASS in round 1, soundness lens REVISE.** The soundness lens caught something the other three (and the drafting pass) missed: the existing `shell-howto` skill already held its own near-verbatim copy of 10 of the 11 moved bullets, with no cross-link to the new hook, AND explicitly claimed the Google Drive note "stays resident in CLAUDE.md" — a claim the edit under review would make false, unflagged, the moment it landed. Fix: rewrote the skill as a pointer-only stub (all duplicated content removed, replaced with pointers to the hook's refs file and to CLAUDE.md for what's still resident). Round 2 re-review: both findings RESOLVED, every claim in the stub individually verified true against the post-change files, no bullet left unhomed.

**Applied + verified:** locked `update-config.ps1 -Op text-replace` for the CLAUDE.md block (exit 0), direct `Write` for the skill stub (not a locked file). Post-apply: direct read confirms only the 3 kept bullets + pointer remain; grep across `.claude/` found zero orphaned references to moved content outside its new home. **49,274 → 45,418 chars** (session cumulative: **51,611 → 45,418, −12.0%** over six clusters/extractions).

**Filed [GEN-336](https://app.notion.com/p/3916e495d07c81f4914ee71bb18750e3)** ("Detect when a reference-injection hook stops delivering its content" — both this hook and the Notion one fail open with no signal if their refs file ever goes missing; propose a `/wrap`-time existence/non-empty check), linked to GEN-334, with the `PreToolUse`-timing correction folded in as a note for a future session to reconcile with GEN-334's body.

**Four-state status:**
- *Done + verified:* hook built, wired, live-fire confirmed; CLAUDE.md block reduced; `shell-howto` skill converted to a stub; GEN-336 filed and re-fetch-verified.
- *Verified-how:* `/check` panel to convergence (2 rounds); post-apply grep + direct read; live hook fire observed in-session (not just a synthetic stdin test).
- *Still open:* the GEN-334 timing-claim correction itself (flagged, not yet edited — GEN-336 carries the note); whether to survey for further compression clusters or call the shrink-half done; all still-open items carried from (5) (size-gate, GEN-333 rescope, Erez's Notion-coverage check, GEN-335's `/check` skill edit); a second cross-link on GEN-336 back to a ticket for this session's own work, once `/wrap` files one.
- *Deliberately not done:* editing GEN-334's body directly this session (Erez chose to fold the correction into GEN-336 and flag it for later rather than act now).

## 2026-07-02 (5) — Cluster 5 merged & applied: the rule-drafting rules (4 rules → 2), 3 real defects caught in round 1, converged in round 2, zero behavior change

Same pipeline as prior clusters this session, on the last identified cluster: the three adjacent "how to draft/revise a rule" rules (positive-phrasing, omit-examples, cross-reference-by-quote) merged into one paragraph, plus a standalone in-place tightening of the "does a new rule clear the bar" rule (no merge, just wording).

**Panel (4 lenses, Sonnet reviewers): round 1 found 3 real, distinct material problems** — the highest-yield round of the session (contrast cluster 3/4's clean round-1 passes). (3) The merged cross-reference clause lost its antecedent — "the rule" landed three clauses deep with no clear subject, a real double-reading risk — caught by the rule-check lens.

**Fixes:** restored the cross-reference clause's full original opening verbatim ("When one rule in a `CLAUDE.md` file references another rule..."), which fixed both the scope-qualifier drop and the antecedent loss in one move and gave the clause its own independent sentence-initial trigger (also resolved a holistic-lens findability concern about burying it mid-paragraph). Kept the broadened trigger (matches its sibling rule's existing "drafting or revising" scope, almost certainly the intended reading) but surfaced it to Erez as an explicit, named behavior change in the approval diff rather than letting it ride silently — Erez approved as presented. Also adopted a reviewer's token-cut: dropped one of two redundant illustrative examples ("the protocol above", since "the rule above/below" already covers the pattern class).

**Round 2 (re-review, all 3 lenses that found issues): all PASS, all findings tagged RESOLVED**, no new issues introduced by the fixes. Holistic lens (round 1) had already passed and confirmed the cluster's scope was correctly bounded — flagged rules at lines 117/119/121 as reasonable *future* merge candidates but correctly left out of this cluster (different trigger conditions, would risk conflating distinct gates).

**Applied + verified:** locked `update-config.ps1 -Op text-replace` (two separate ops in one script), exit 0 confirmed for both; grep confirmed new merged text present verbatim, no leftover old three-rule phrasing. **49,407 → 49,277 chars** (session cumulative: **51,611 → 49,277 chars, −4.5%** over five clusters, all zero-unflagged-behavior-change and reviewer-verified).

- *Still open:* everything carried in (4)'s still-open list (shell block awaiting GEN-334, size-gate, GEN-333 rescope, skill cleanup, Erez's Notion-coverage check, GEN-335's `/check` skill edit). No further compression clusters currently queued — Erez to decide whether to survey for more or consider this workstream's shrink-half complete for now.

Continuation of 2026-07-02 (1)'s "shrink half" (Lever 1: compress/merge the always-resident behavioral rules in place — every rule stays resident and followed; only tokens are cut). Erez chose this over the shell reference block, and moved the session to **Fable-5 at high effort** for it.

**Shell block deliberately deferred (decision):** the Notion hook works because Notion work announces itself in the prompt; shell work usually arises mid-turn with no prompt cue, so a `UserPromptSubmit` hook is the wrong trigger for it. All dangerous shell notes stay resident regardless.

**Cluster 1 — ticket-status (4 rules → 1, ~430 → ~280 words).** The four interlocking rules (propose-and-wait; primary-ticket auto-set; re-read-title-before-Done; the 4-branch verification ladder) spent ~90 words cross-referencing each other; merged into one two-paragraph rule. Panel round 1 caught a real scope leak in my draft — the verification bullets lost Rule D's per-bullet "propose" verb, which could have licensed self-setting Review on a non-primary ticket (or skipping the ladder for non-primary tickets); fixed by hoisting the mood assignment ("for the primary ticket set the result directly, for any other ticket propose it") above the ladder. Converged round 2; Erez then asked to split the dense opening sentence (round 3 PASS) and caught that "— primary or not —" (added by me as emphasis) was redundant — dropped after a delta check (round 4 PASS). Applied, verified: 51,611 → 50,811 chars.

Root cause: reviewer briefs define "material" as behavior-change/harm and demote wording preferences to advisory — so in a compression-goal task, redundant words sail through. Direction: goal-relative materiality in briefs + a "does every word earn its place" pass. (Applied immediately in-session: cluster 2's briefs included goal-relative materiality, and its panel did flag a redundant parenthetical — the fix works.)

**Cluster 2 — question-handling (2 rules → 1, ~260 → ~185 words).** The "questions are analysis requests, not authorization" rule + the "ask clarifying questions first / run `/check` before presenting" rule. First panel run was interrupted; Erez set high effort and asked for a restart — redrafted from scratch. Round 1 caught: the `/check` duty lost its "Either way" connector (could be read as only covering the no-clarifying-needed branch); the answer-first override was no longer explicit. Also resolved one reviewer claim **by evidence against the original text**: the wait-before-acting duty was always scoped to questions (Rule 1's own trigger), so keeping it question-scoped is faithful — not a loss. Extra cut taken: "one question at a time" dropped from the merged rule (the standalone global rule covers it universally — two reviewers confirmed the delegation safe). One advisory adopted ("default" → "order" so the override can't be read as cancelling the wait duty; delta check PASS). Applied, verified: 50,811 → 50,339 chars.

## 2026-07-02 (1) — "CLAUDE.md too large to follow" solved reliably: detection-layer reframe + a *verified* prompt-time injection hook; Notion notes moved out of the always-loaded file

Continued the 2026-07-01 (4) problem (global `CLAUDE.md` too large to reliably follow). Erez pushed for a *reliable* fix ("millions face this; Anthropic recommend keeping it small") rather than another guess.

**Got ground truth this time (no memory-guessing).** Read Anthropic's official memory doc (`code.claude.com/docs/en/memory`) directly + ran a 78-agent research→adversarial-verification workflow, then `/check` (3 lenses, Opus, converged 2 rounds). Key verified facts: CLAUDE.md loads in full every session; "shorter files produce better adherence" but size is **one of four** adherence drivers (also specificity/structure/consistency) — so it's directional, **not** the reliability fix; `@import` expands at launch (no size saving); the doc explicitly says *"to block an action regardless of what Claude decides, use a PreToolUse hook."*

**The reframe (the real answer):** the goal has two halves needing different mechanisms. **(A) Reliable following** = a *detection/enforcement* layer independent of file size: hooks for deterministic gates + the GEN-333 auto-review trip-wire (promoted to first-class). **(B) Shrinking** = maintainability hygiene that helps adherence only at the margin — explicitly NOT the reliability fix. `/check`'s round-1 pre-mortem forced this split (I'd implied shrinking delivers reliability); also forced a **hard write-time budget gate** (not another soft rule) for anti-regrowth, and diff-based compression to avoid silently dropping load-bearing qualifiers.

**REVERSAL of 2026-07-01 (4):** last session concluded "hooks inject reliably only at SessionStart/Stop, not before a tool call," which (with skills-don't-auto-load) killed relocation. This session tested the mechanism I'd never tried: a **`UserPromptSubmit` hook**. Docs + a live fresh-session test (Erez ran it; marker `MARIGOLD-7431` received; I also saw it injected in my own turn) confirm **`UserPromptSubmit` `additionalContext` injects deterministically on this machine**. That resurrects relocation — but via a *reliable* channel (hook), not the coin-flip skills. (`PreToolUse` still injects only *after* the call — that limit stands; it's the GEN-334 gap.)

**Built + shipped (Notion domain, end-to-end):**
- `hooks/inject-notion-refs.js` (`UserPromptSubmit`) + `hooks/refs/notion.md` (the 7 safe Notion notes). Hook detects Notion signals in the prompt (word "notion", `GEN-\d+`, notion.so, `notion-*` tool names, Team-Tasks, `collection://`) and injects the notes; silent otherwise. Tested through its real stdin interface (Notion prompt → injects; non-Notion → silent), wired into `settings.json` under `UserPromptSubmit` via the locked `update-config.ps1`. Erez confirmed it fires on real Notion prompts in a fresh session.
- **Then deleted** the 7 non-dangerous Notion notes from global `CLAUDE.md` (via `update-config.ps1 -Op text-replace`, exit 0, synced). Block went 10 lines → 3: kept the **`notion-duplicate-page` safety note** verbatim (silent-damage-on-miss → stays resident) + a pointer line to the hook. Verified by re-read; no dangling cross-refs (checked); ~2.9K chars removed with zero coverage loss.

**Filed:** [GEN-334](https://app.notion.com/p/3916e495d07c819fb748f4b0213d1cff) — "Cover mid-turn Notion work with the reference-injection hook (PreToolUse backstop)" (GEN-86, Backlog, unassigned, task template). Captures that `UserPromptSubmit` only sees the prompt, so mid-turn Notion work (no prompt cue) isn't covered; a `PreToolUse` backstop would help but only injects after the first call. Erez chose to accept the gap now + log it (option a); no cross-link (Team-Tasks has only `Parent item`; see-also goes in body text — none needed here).

**Superseded from 2026-07-01 (4):** the "delete the two skill files" recommendation is moot — those skills weren't used; the reliable path is a *hook*, not a skill. Erez also caught my self-contradiction (I'd called the skills unreliable, then called deleting-their-resident-copy "safe") → dropped skill-based relocation entirely; shrinking now never routes content behind a coin-flip mechanism.

**Four-state status:**
- *Done + verified:* `UserPromptSubmit` injection verified live; Notion hook built, wired, live-confirmed by Erez; 7 Notion notes deleted from `CLAUDE.md` (exit 0, re-read confirms, safety note intact).
- *Verified-how:* fresh-session marker test; hook run through real stdin interface; post-edit re-read + grep for the retained safety note.
- *Still open:* shell reference block (same hook-then-delete pattern, next domain); the **hard size-limit gate** in `update-config.ps1` (anti-regrowth, not built); GEN-333 auto-review (the other reliability half); fate of now-redundant `notion-howto`/`shell-howto` skills; compress/merge the ~50 always-resident behavioral rules (maintainability half); Erez's own final sanity-check of Notion coverage in a future real-work session.
- *Deliberately not done:* GEN-334's PreToolUse backstop (gap accepted for now); touched nothing beyond the Notion block.

Unresolved items filed: GEN-334 (mid-turn backstop). Still-open follow-ups above (shell block, size-gate, GEN-333 rescope, skill-file cleanup, behavioral-rule compression) are not yet ticketed — carry to `/wrap`.

## 2026-07-01 (4) — GEN-332 → reframed into "CLAUDE.md too large to follow"; three shrink mechanisms killed; shrink-via-skills rejected by /check; landed on delete/compress + GEN-333 auto-review; GEN-333 filed

Started on [GEN-332](https://app.notion.com/p/3906e495d07c81bcbc14d253354f2875) ("Class E over-engineering pattern — decide sharpen-rule vs accept"; see 2026-07-01 (3) for how it was filed). Researched + `/check`ed a recommendation to **accept the residual** (grounded in the trend: Class E is flat-to-slightly-declining over the log's ~5-week life, not rising; the net catches it every time). `/check` converged in 2 rounds — but flagged that my first pass was *itself* a Class-E instance (collapsed the option space to a false binary, argued asymmetrically toward the cheap answer). Never got to apply the GEN-332 recommendation, because:

**Erez reframed the whole thing.** He rejected "accept vs sharpen" as the same dead-end and named the real problem: **the global `CLAUDE.md` (~13,700 tokens, ~80 rules) has grown so large I don't reliably follow it** — so adding/sharpening rules makes it worse. Diagnosed (via a live specimen — my own GEN-332 slip) that this is an **attention/retrieval failure, not a coherence failure**: the four rules that should have stopped my slip were all present and correct; I skimmed past them anyway. So the lever isn't better rule *wording* — it's making the right rules reach me at the right moment.

**Explored the fix space, then killed three mechanisms in turn (each verified, not assumed):**
1. **Move bulky rules → skills that auto-load on relevance.** Verified via `claude-code-guide` + a fresh-session test Erez ran: skills do **NOT** auto-load — a skill body loads only on *explicit* invocation (`/name` or a model-emitted Skill call); the resident one-line description never pulls the body in. Both test skills failed to auto-load on realistic cues. **Dead.**
2. **A `PreToolUse` hook that injects the notes right before Notion/shell tool calls.** Verified against docs + the machine's own hooks (`permission-denied-log.js`/`stop-cred-denial-surface.js` design notes): hooks inject reliably only at **SessionStart or Stop (end-of-turn)**, not "before a specific tool call." PreToolUse `additionalContext` timing is ambiguous/likely-post-call. **Not viable for shaping the call.**
3. **A resident one-line "signpost" that tells me to invoke the skill** (Erez's suggestion — is invoking more reliable than auto-load? yes, but…). `/check` (4 lenses, Opus) unanimously rejected the whole **signpost+skill shrink** as **net-negative for the goal**: the signpost is *circular* (the goal is "I don't follow resident rules"; the fix is a new resident rule saying "go read more rules"); it trades a **visible** failure (skim past a present rule, recoverable) for a **silent** one (skill never invoked → note absent → no signal); it shrinks the **least-relevant** section (the tool-gotchas, not the ~177 lines of behavioral rules actually implicated); the token saving is **illusory** (if the signpost works, the skill loads and the tokens re-enter context — only Notion/shell-free sessions save). The file's **own rule** (a skill is only the right home for something that fires "when the user invokes a specific, named task") says these model-triggered notes belong resident.

**Landed on (recommendation to Erez, not yet actioned):** abandon shrink-via-skills; delete the two now-dead skill files (`notion-howto`, `shell-howto` — created this session, never wired in, nothing deleted from `CLAUDE.md` so no damage); if the file must shrink, do a **delete-dead + compress-verbose-in-place** pass (shrinks the behavioral section that matters, zero reliability cost, no relocation/invocation gate — the strictly-dominant lever the panel surfaced and I'd failed to consider); and **promote [GEN-333](https://app.notion.com/p/3906e495d07c81c39adae838f7ae4d70) (the automatic end-of-turn `/check` trigger) from fallback to the primary fix** — it's the only lever that catches slips regardless of file size. **Awaiting Erez's pick (auto-catch-primary+delete/compress vs delete/compress-only vs pause).**

**Filed:** [GEN-333](https://app.notion.com/p/3906e495d07c81c39adae838f7ae4d70) — "Add an automatic independent-review trip-wire for recommendation/design turns" (GEN-86, Backlog, unassigned; Task template). Filed as a *fallback* mid-session; per the above it should be re-scoped to *primary* (not yet edited on the ticket).

**GEN-58 Class-D recurrence logged** (element: "assert Claude Code token/loading mechanics from memory"): the entire Option-3 design — and my own `/check` panel — rested on the unverified premise that skills auto-load their body on a context cue. Erez's prove-before-delete gate + the fresh-session test caught it before any deletion. Wrote a dated trace on [Vol. 3](https://app.notion.com/p/38a6e495d07c8123a071e840880f0510) and bumped Class D on [GEN-58](https://app.notion.com/p/36d6e495d07c816e9e0cce265d694ab3) (38x→39x, last 2026-07-01); both writes verified.

**Meta (worth carrying forward):** three times this session the elaborate mechanism (skills → hook → signpost) lost to a simpler one (delete/compress; catch slips after the fact) under independent review, and the simpler answer was available from the start. The `/check` panel caught it each time; first-pass instinct kept missing it — a persistent Class-E signal that ties directly back to GEN-332's open question.

**Four-state status:** GEN-332 recommendation *researched + `/check`ed* but **not applied** (superseded by the reframe — still open: the accept-vs-sharpen decision, now subsumed into the bigger CLAUDE.md-size question). Three shrink mechanisms *verified dead*. Shrink-via-skills *rejected by `/check`*. Delete/compress + GEN-333-primary *recommended, awaiting Erez's decision — deliberately not actioned*. Two skill files *created, now recommended for deletion — not yet deleted*.

Unresolved items filed: GEN-333 (needs re-scope to primary + a build decision); GEN-332 (accept-vs-sharpen, now folded into the CLAUDE.md-size decision); the delete/compress pass + skill-file cleanup await Erez's pick.

## 2026-06-30 (2) — GEN-322 bootstrap done; live Credential Manager lookup verified; SKILL.md fixed on disk

Continued from session (9) — the `notion-ticket-lookup` SKILL.md had been designed but the on-disk file was still the old 77-line pre-Credential-Manager version; the bootstrap ticket ([GEN-322](https://app.notion.com/p/38e6e495d07c814b8449f7fbb8289aaa)) was still open (assigned to Erez).

**GEN-322 bootstrap (Erez):** Erez ran the one-time bootstrap to store the Notion API token in Windows Credential Manager (`PasswordVault`, service/username both `claude-notion-token`). First attempt stored a placeholder (`YOUR-NOTION-TOKEN-HERE`) — identified via blank `Write-Host $v2.Password` output. Fix: `$vault.Remove($cred)`, re-add with the real token, verified with `$v3.RetrievePassword()`. GEN-322 marked Done by Erez.

**Live end-to-end test — two sequential failures, both fixed:**
1. `invalid_json` — PS 5.1 mangles embedded quotes in `-d '{"filter":...}'` when passed to a native exe (`curl.exe`). Fix: write body to `$env:TEMP\notion-body.json` via `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` + `--data-binary "@$bodyFile"`.
2. `object_not_found` — SKILL.md had the MCP `collection://` UUID (`bd2cd17b-...`) as the database id, which is NOT the REST API id. Fix: derived correct REST id from `GET /v1/databases/` → `fe198002-6618-48d7-ae04-56f8cee479f3` (the `Team-Tasks` database). After both fixes, the live query returned the GEN-313 page URL correctly.

**SKILL.md rewritten on disk** (77 → 117 lines): primary single-id path now uses the Credential Manager REST `unique_id` query; both fixes embedded (temp-file body, correct REST db id with a staleness/re-derive note); `object_not_found` re-derive path documented; four pinned anchors (GEN-58, GEN-86, GEN-21, GEN-62); security rules (never `-v`, never echo `$token`).

**Global `CLAUDE.md` — two Notion gotcha rules added** (both via `update-config.ps1`, exit 0):
- `collection://` UUID ≠ REST API database id — always derive from `ancestor-2-database`.
- curl body in PowerShell — never inline `-d`, use temp file + `--data-binary "@$bodyFile"`.

**GEN-58 Class D entry filed** — two unverified platform primitives baked into the SKILL.md (wrong UUID, wrong curl pattern) caused a fix-on-fix cascade; new element D.x added to [GEN-58 Vol. 3](https://app.notion.com/p/38a6e495d07c8123a071e840880f0510); index updated (count 36→37, date 2026-06-30).

## 2026-06-30 (1) — GEN-323 resolved: bar-check timing (apply-time for learnings, proposal-time for direct proposals)

Resolved [GEN-323](https://app.notion.com/p/38e6e495d07c81c0bb37fa7874a48691) — the open question filed in entry (10): the global rule "Before proposing a new rule, first confirm it clears the bar" never said *when* the check fires. Under the new learning workflow there are two moments — surfacing-time (a learning is noticed / filed as a Notion ticket; `/wrap` files generously) and apply-time (a later session turns the learning into a rule/skill/hook).

**Decision:** asked Erez; he chose **apply-time** (recommended) — `/wrap` files generously by design, and the bar is really about what becomes a *standing* always-loaded rule, where the per-session cost lands. He agreed.

**The `/check` pass (converged, 2 rounds, 4 lenses round 1 then pre-mortem+rule-check re-reviews):**
- Round 1 draft framed the firing point purely around learnings ("fires at apply-time … not at surfacing-time"). Soundness + Holistic PASSed (advisories: trim "deliberately generous"; align the trigger word "proposing"); **pre-mortem AND rule-check independently caught the same material defect** — the learning-only framing would read as exempting *all* first-notice moments from the bar-check, including rules proposed **directly** mid-session (which have no surfacing/apply split), silently dropping the check for that common path.
- Round 2 revision scoped the apply-time clause to learning-originated rules and added an explicit sentence for direct proposals ("checked at proposal-time, before drafting rule text"); folded in both advisories. Both re-review lenses tagged the finding RESOLVED, no new issues. **Converged.**

**Applied** (line 121, via `update-global-rule.ps1`, exit 0 + synced; verified by reading the line back). Wrote the two em-dashes as ASCII `--` to avoid the known non-ASCII mangling risk on this machine — wording/meaning identical to the converged text.

**Tickets:** GEN-323 → **Done** (moved manually by Erez after the fix landed). No new unresolved items this session — the two `/check` findings were both resolved in-session.

**Config-health (global auto scan):** global changed since marker `774fe2c` (this session's line-121 edit + two Notion-gotcha bullets added by a separate session `f83ca686` today); scanned the changed rules against the whole file — no redundancy/contradiction/dangling/stale findings (the one positional-cross-ref hit is the cross-reference rule quoting the banned phrase to define it — false positive, dropped). ## 2026-06-29 (8) — Closed a `sync.ps1` backup gap for the two GEN-316 hooks

Two Claude Code hook scripts created earlier for [GEN-316](https://app.notion.com/p/38d6e495d07c814eb9a1eba96f8b96fa) (credential-denial detector) — `permission-denied-log.js` (PermissionDenied hook) and `stop-cred-denial-surface.js` (Stop hook) — were registered in `settings.json` but absent from `sync.ps1`'s explicit `$FileMap`, so they were not backed up to Drive or git history. `settings.json` thus referenced two scripts that existed only on the local machine — a backup gap.

Fix: added both as `$FileMap` hashtable entries (Drive/Claude/Git paths, following the existing `hooks\*.js` pattern) and documented them in the header comment block. Ran `sync.ps1 -Direction From-Claude` via the PowerShell tool (its host has Process-scope Bypass for the `G:` mount).

Built the marker design that was vetted in session (3) and approved by Erez. Added to [`update-config.ps1`](G:\My Drive\AI Projects\_Tooling\Claude\update-config.ps1):

- **Exit code 5 documented** in the script header's exit-code list.

Policy Part 2: before this session `CurrentUser=Undefined`, `LocalMachine=RemoteSigned`; the Bypass set deliberately supersedes a stray `RemoteSigned` I'd set earlier on the first wrong theory. **Captured:** a new PowerShell-cluster bullet in global `CLAUDE.md`, `/check`-converged over 2 rounds (4 lenses — pre-mortem/holistic/soundness/rule-check); the panel reframed "the durable fix" → options with the security tradeoff + a confirm-with-Erez gate, removed a time-varying "(applied on this machine)" phrase and an auto-classifier sentence, and grounded the Bash-tool claim in the child-process-inheritance invariant. Erez then asked to settle, once and for all, why the lookup keeps failing and why a "fix from earlier today" wasn't applied.

**Diagnosis (root-caused via session-transcript search, `C:\Users\Erez\.claude\projects\C--Users-Erez\*.jsonl`):**
- The "we fixed it today" memory was partly real, partly not. In session `eddc326e` today, Erez said "gen 123" and that session **did** resolve it — via the Notion REST `POST /v1/databases/<db-id>/query` with `{"filter":{"property":"ID","unique_id":{"equals":123}}}` (numeric part only) — **but** it worked by **inlining the literal token** into the PowerShell command. That is the leak, not a clean fix.
- A *separate* session (`be9c3055`) only **analysed** a "TOC for Notion tickets to save tokens" question, concluded a TOC wouldn't help, and ended with `/wrap` having **changed no files**. So the better method was *identified* but never *written into the skill*. The skill was last edited 06-28, not today.
- Corrected two of Erez's premises on the merits (not deferring): (a) "the API sheet leaks tokens into conversation" → the **sheet is not the leak**; reading the token is fine. The real leak path (from session `9307c1c7`) is a token-bearing command getting **allow-listed**, which saves the literal command — token and all — into `settings.local.json`, which syncs to Drive + is committed to the config git repo. (b) Confirmed the inlined-token command in `eddc326e` did **not** surface the token to Erez on screen, but it **was** written into the session record.

**Verified live constraints (read `hooks/auto-approve.js` + `secret-patterns.json`):** the auto-approve hook does NOT classify/deny credential commands — the "[Credential Leakage]" block is Claude Code's built-in **auto-mode classifier** (separate layer), which refuses a command with a literal credential inline. The hook's only credential handling is `redactSecrets()` applied **before writing to the deferred-calls log** (GEN-233); `secret-patterns.json` already covers the Notion token (`(ntn_|secret_)[A-Za-z0-9]{36,}`). `notion-fetch`/`notion-search` are in `SAFE_TOOLS` (token-free, auto-approved) but can't map a number→page. `notion-query-data-sources` is plan-gated here (not usable today).

**Design (ran `/check`, 3 lenses — pre-mortem/holistic/soundness; converged in 2 rounds, all PASS round 2):** The first draft (plaintext token file in `.claude-secrets\`) was rejected on material findings: (1) **circular bootstrap** — the design ruled out Claude reading the token, yet "populate the file by reading the sheet" required exactly that (no non-Claude actor named); (2) "zero prompts via allow-list" conflated a manual allow-list add with auto-approval (the command defers — `curl.exe` and `$env:`-segments aren't in the safe-set); (3) the "token never in context" guarantee was fragile (breaks under `curl -v`; token lands on curl.exe's process command line); (4) **holistic** — a plaintext file is a net-new cleartext copy when **Windows Credential Manager** gives the same allow-list/classifier-safety property DPAPI-encrypted. **Converged design (v2):** store the token in **Credential Manager** (native `[Windows.Security.Credentials.PasswordVault]` API — the `CredentialManager` PS module isn't installed; **verified the native API works on this machine with a dummy value: store+retrieve+remove, test entry cleaned up**); **bootstrap is one-time and done BY EREZ out of band** (his own PowerShell window; Claude only prepares a placeholder snippet — the token never enters a Claude session); per-lookup command retrieves the token into a process-local var, runs `curl.exe -sk` (NEVER `-v`) on the `unique_id` query, returns only the URL, then `notion-fetch` opens the page; optional one-time allow-list add for zero prompts (stored text references the store, not the token); plus expand the pinned-anchor list to cut REST frequency. Accepted residual risk: the token is briefly on curl.exe's local process command line (local-machine trust model; strictly better than the status quo).

**State (four-state):**
- **Done/verified:** root-cause diagnosis (verified against transcripts); the secure design (`/check`-converged); the native PasswordVault mechanism (verified live with a dummy value).
- **Saved durably:** converged design + ordered build steps → `skills/notion-ticket-lookup/SECURE-LOOKUP-DESIGN.md` (so a Sonnet session can build without re-deriving; the session scratchpad copy is session-specific and wouldn't survive a model switch).
- **Still open (the build, deferred to a Sonnet session):** (1) EREZ runs the one-time Credential-Manager bootstrap; (2) rewrite `skills/notion-ticket-lookup/SKILL.md` so the primary single-id path is pinned-anchor → Credential-Manager-backed REST → ask Erez, with the non-secret Team-Tasks DB id cached + a staleness/re-derive note; (3) optional one-time allow-list add; (4) expand pinned anchors.
- **Deliberately not done:** GEN-313 itself still unresolved (blocked on the fix or on Erez pasting its URL); **no ticket filed yet** for this work (offered; Erez moved to `/loghistory` first) — a Notion-platform-domain ticket for the lookup mechanism is the natural home; **no git commit/sync** (belongs to `/wrap`).
- **Dead end not worth retrying:** reading the token from the gsheet via the Drive MCP to bootstrap — it necessarily surfaces the value into Claude's context (the 4th leak surface), so the bootstrap must be out-of-band.

Single-ticket session: look up **[GEN-317](https://app.notion.com/p/38e6e495d07c81a4898fde80a7045191)** ("Auto-deploy after approved implementation", under GEN-86, Backlog/Low), then build the rule it proposes. - **Self-defeating exception (pre-mortem, sharp):** the first draft's exception "takes effect immediately for users in a sensitive way" would fire on *every* `clasp push` (which is always immediately live), making the rule vacuous for the very case that prompted it. Verified by reading the live file. This dissolved several round-1 pre-mortem findings.

Erez chose to cut one further (redundant, non-blocking) sentence the panel flagged. **Applied** the converged text to global `CLAUDE.md` (last rule in the "Instructions — Apply to All Chats" section) via `update-global-rule.ps1` (exit 0); re-read the file to confirm it landed (ellipsis + em-dashes intact, no encoding mangling). **[GEN-317](https://app.notion.com/p/38e6e495d07c81a4898fde80a7045191) → Done** (re-fetch-verified).

## 2026-06-29 (3) — Vetted GEN-306 via /check before building; its own fix was unsafe; converged on a marker design and rewrote the ticket

Session was a single request: look up **[GEN-306](https://app.notion.com/p/3896e495d07c81d2bb9dc4663037e596)** ("Add a dirty-state guard to `update-config.ps1` so its pre-edit Drive pull can't clobber un-synced local edits", under GEN-86, Backlog/Medium), then — when Erez asked "is it vetted and ready, or should we `/check` it first?" — run `/check` on the proposed work and act on the outcome. No code was written this session; GEN-306 stays **Backlog** with a vetted design.

**Round 1 `/check` (3 lenses: pre-mortem, holistic, soundness) — all REVISE.** The ticket's own proposed fix was *compare each managed file's local content against the **Drive** copy; on divergence stop*. The panel killed it on two material flaws self-review (mine) missed:
- **False divergence off the live Drive mount** — `G:\My Drive\…` is Google Drive File Stream, constantly syncing; a file caught mid-download hashes differently from both old and new state, so the guard would jam the config tool with false alarms.
- **Can't classify the divergence** — "local ≠ Drive" can't distinguish *local-ahead* (genuine un-synced edit, must not clobber) from *Drive-ahead* (another machine pushed; local is stale and the pull is *supposed* to overwrite it). The guard would block the very pull it's meant to allow. Soundness reviewer proposed the **git-history backup** (`C:\Users\Erez\.claude-git-history\`, the last-confirmed-synced state) as the correct baseline.

**Round 2 `/check` on the git-history-baseline design — converged the answer onto a simpler mechanism.** Soundness: PASS (advisories only — exit code 5 is free; compare the working-tree file on disk, not committed HEAD). Holistic + pre-mortem: REVISE, but the findings were *refinements*, and the pre-mortem reviewer surfaced (and holistic endorsed) a strictly simpler design: a **pending-sync marker**. The git-history baseline has a real fragility — it only updates at the *end* of a successful push, and a push can succeed to Drive but fail to finish updating the backup (deliberately tolerated), leaving the baseline stale → then it false-blocks *every* future edit with no auto-recovery.

**Decision point surfaced to Erez (the hinge):** the marker design only catches edits made *through* `update-config.ps1`; the git-history design also catches out-of-tool edits. Asked Erez how the original clobbered `SKILL.md` was edited → **"via that tool."** That settles it: **pending-sync marker** is final (simpler, no dependence on background-syncing state, can't false-alarm off a network hiccup, covers the motivating incident). Verified against the real script flow that the marker closes the exact sequence: write file (Step 3) → push fails (Step 4 already exits 4 here) → marker survives → next run sees it and refuses the pull. Marker must be written before Step 3 and removed only on the Step-4 success path — **not** in the `finally` (which runs on failure too).

**Rewrote GEN-306 description** (approved by Erez) to the marker approach via `notion-update-page replace_content`; re-fetched and verified the full new body landed. Title kept ("dirty-state guard" still fits a marker); status stays **Backlog**. Known-limitation (won't catch out-of-tool edits) recorded as accepted, since the PreToolUse hook blocks direct edits anyway.

**GEN-58 (Class-D recurrence, →36×, last 2026-06-29):** trace added to [Vol. 3](https://app.notion.com/p/38a6e495d07c8123a071e840880f0510); index count bumped 35→36 (verified by re-fetch). Classified as a recurrence (not a new element) of "designed/diagnosed on an unverified primitive" — I presented the ticket's mechanism as plausibly ready instead of treating it as a draft to verify; same shape and same subsystem as the 2026-06-24 D element `[update-config pull clobbers local skill edit]` that spawned this very ticket. Lesson already captured: a ticket's proposed mechanism is a draft to verify, not a vetted spec.

**Still open / not done this session:** GEN-306 is *not* built — implementing the marker (a code change to the locked config tooling) is its own task; offered to Erez (with a move to In Progress) but not yet taken up. No `CLAUDE.md`/memory/git/config-sync changes (those belong to `/wrap`).

## 2026-06-29 — Dropped the model/effort self-fitness rule from global CLAUDE.md

Session opened with project selection ("3" = Improve AI Infra). After a brief Track A status recap (see below), the real work was retiring a rule Erez flagged as not working.

**Trigger (Erez):** "We already worked on getting you to tell me when to switch model or effort level, but it almost never happens, and I have to do it on my own. Why is that?"

**Diagnosis (agreed):** the global `CLAUDE.md` rule "at the start of every response, check that the model and effort level fit the request — flag any mismatch and suggest the adjustment" almost never fired. Root cause is structural, not wording — it's a **self-policing behavioral rule with no enforcement trigger**: depends on me choosing to run the check every turn, competes with the task (which wins), has no crisp "if X then flag" threshold (judging my own output mid-production is the worst vantage point), and biases toward silence. A behavioral rule can't deliver a "dependable nudge" goal — that needs an enforcement mechanism (a hook reading the prompt) or the user's own judgment. Same lesson as the existing "rule vs skill vs hook home-test."

**Decision (Erez): drop the rule.** Scope confirmed one question at a time: drop the main rule + both dependents — the `Model guide` (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku task-type list) and the "/compact-first when downgrading in a long conversation" follow-on. All four pieces (global `CLAUDE.md` lines 35–44) removed.

**How applied / verified:**
- Confirmed first (via Grep) that no other rule in the file referenced the deleted lines — no dangling refs.
- Deleted via the locked-config tool `update-config.ps1 -Op text-replace` with an empty `-NewText` (the `update-global-rule.ps1` wrapper can't delete — requires non-empty NewText). First attempt failed (**exit 2** — the tool requires `-NewText` to be *present*); re-ran passing `-NewText ''` explicitly → **exit 0**, synced to Drive + git. OldText was verified byte-identical to the source block (`cmp`) before running, to avoid the non-ASCII em-dash/arrow matching risk.
- A second tiny `text-replace` collapsed a double blank line the deletion left behind (exit 0). Verified the final join with `sed`/`cat -A`: exactly one blank line between the surviving rules.

**GEN-58 (Class-G recurrence, →14×, last 2026-06-29):** trace added to [Vol. 3](https://app.notion.com/p/38a6e495d07c8123a071e840880f0510); count bumped in the index. Classified as a recurrence of the `[GEN-277 home-test mis-scope]` element (a rule added without applying the rule/skill/hook home-test), **not** a new element. Flagged honestly that this was *not* a live this-session failure caught by pushback — I diagnosed and dropped it cleanly — so it's a retrospective recurrence logged only for the persistence signal; Erez confirmed logging it anyway.

**[GEN-171](https://app.notion.com/p/3756e495d07c81758548e6646ad84a53) (Rule currency & enforcement, To Do — unchanged):** appended a "Retired rule — model/effort self-fitness check (2026-06-29)" section recording what was removed, why, and a **"do not re-add as prose"** note — if the felt need returns, the only durable form is an enforcement hook, not another always-loaded rule. ## 2026-06-28 (6) — GEN-312 event-driven design resolved -> decided NOT to build (Wont Do)

Continued the GEN-312 auto-verify-robot design on Opus, focused on Erez's event-driven (push, not poll) requirement.

**Mechanism check:** the platform *can* fire a Routine on demand — the remote-trigger `run` action launches it instantly, not only on a schedule — so push-triggering is technically possible.

**Grounding via the real backlog:** classified all 15 tickets then in **Review** into three trigger kinds:
- **Bucket 1 — Claude self-verifies** (check is already part of a `/wrap` or `/config-health` run): GEN-271, GEN-290, GEN-299.
- **Bucket 2 — machine result, auto-checkable by polling a log** (invoice daily-run outcomes): GEN-194, GEN-215, GEN-289.
- **Bucket 3 — needs Erez, no catchable signal** (subjective observation / manual UI tap / pending decision): GEN-76, GEN-147, GEN-154, GEN-204, GEN-247, GEN-294; GEN-280 mixed (machine half + human-gated half).
- Already verified → closed this session: GEN-212, GEN-260.
A trigger-class comment was logged on each of the 15.

**Key finding:** the push-event premise doesn't survive the real backlog. Bucket 3 (the majority) can't be event-driven at all (no signal — only Erez); Bucket 1 already self-verifies via existing `/wrap`/`/config-health` runs; Bucket 2 is poll-the-log, not push. The robot would be two unrelated jobs (poll invoice logs + nudge Erez), with Bucket 1 already handled.

**Decision (Erez): do NOT build the system.** GEN-312 → **Wont Do**; full discussion + decision logged in the ticket body. Not reworked further, per Erez.

**Closures:** GEN-212 → Done (Confluence add-on verified end-to-end; the referenced PlayFab-config security exposure was alerted to dev and assessed a **non-issue**; the `C:\fp-test` scaffold was already gone). GEN-260 → Done (duplicate-filing fix live-verified 2026-06-17).

**Notion free-plan access (confirmed):** the status-filtered query is reachable only via the Notion REST API (`POST /v1/databases/{id}/query`) using `$env:NOTION_TOKEN` from the secure sheet — the MCP query/view tools are all Business-plan-gated and semantic search can't filter by status. The auto-mode classifier blocked the token-bearing call repeatedly in *this* session (after several credential-handling attempts accumulated in context), while a parallel clean session ran the identical call and returned the 15 — evidence the classifier weighs conversational context.

**Follow-ups (resolved later this session):**

## 2026-06-28 (5) — GEN-309 second-/compact block: misleading message fixed, safety kept

[GEN-309](https://app.notion.com/p/38a6e495d07c8102abd4faba9d25c780) (Bug, under GEN-86) → **Done** (QA'd automatically only; not yet on a real live `/compact`).

**Confirmed root cause** (the ticket's prior stale-`CLAUDE_SESSION_ID` theory was already retracted): the gate file `/loghistory` writes (`.loggate-ok-<id>`) is correctly named, but `sessionstart-loggate.js` deletes it on every session segment — **including `source: "compact"`** (the intentional per-segment reset). So after the first `/compact` the flag is gone and the next `/compact` blocks. Verified `SessionStart` re-fires post-compact with `source: "compact"` (Claude Code docs, via claude-code-guide). The block happens regardless of session-id stability.

**Decision:** the per-segment reset is correct and was **kept** — after a compact the model sees only a summary and can't log the prior detail, so each compact should be preceded by a fresh log. The only real defect was the block message, which falsely said "this session isn't logged yet" on a second compact.

**Fix:** reworded the `PreCompact` `reason` string in `precompact-loggate.js` (gate logic untouched). `/check`-converged in 1 round (pre-mortem + soundness + holistic, all PASS; advisories non-blocking). Verified: `node --check`, plus a live hook run emitting the new message.

**Regression test:** added `hooks/tests/gen309-gate-test.sh` — drives the real hooks through the full sequence (startup → unlogged compact blocks → log → compact allowed → post-compact reset deletes flag → second compact blocks with the new message) using a throwaway session id; 12/12 pass. Anti-rot: pointer comments added to the top of both `precompact-loggate.js` and `sessionstart-loggate.js` telling anyone editing the gate to re-run it.
